> **`watcher-claude-john-showcase-12chapters-2026-05-27` — FINISHED 2026-05-27 — Gospel of John pushed from 6→12 chapters as the showcase corpus.** Per John (the user): "push it on Christian now to populate more. pick books and fill in to use as example, use one famous gospel like john". 6 new SCRIPTURE_TEXTS entries added under docNode phase-3-020-gospel-of-john: john-2 (Cana water-into-wine, the first sign · NRSV + Brown Anchor + Greek), john-4 (Samaritan woman at the well, "God is spirit" + "worship in spirit and truth"), john-8 ("Before Abraham was, I AM" — the central Christological self-claim, locus classicus of NT high Christology via egō eimi linking back to LXX Exod 3:14), john-11 (Lazarus raised + "I am the resurrection and the life" — the climactic seventh sign, structural hinge of the Fourth Gospel), john-14 ("I am the way, the truth, and the life" — the most-debated single sentence in NT comparative-religion theology, with explicit Bahá'í Íqán cross-tradition link to the temporally-relativised re-reading), john-19 (the crucifixion + "It is finished" / tetelestai, with hyssop-Passover typology). Lighter density throughout — 1-2 sections × 1 verse + Greek NA28 originals + 3-5 crossTradition each. T1 translations throughout (NRSV, Brown Anchor Bible John I-XII / XIII-XXI, Brown Death of the Messiah). Cross-tradition spines wired: LXX Exod 3:14 egō eimi → John 8:58; John 14:6 → Bahá'í Íqán temporally-relativised reading; Isaiah 53 + 4 Maccabees 6:29 antipsychon → John 19 passion theology; Psalm 22 → all four passion narratives; Ezekiel 37 + 1 Cor 15 → John 11 resurrection theology. **Plus two architectural fixes uncovered while testing**: (1) `_annotate` in scripture-reader.js was vulnerable to overlapping entity-word breakage — a shorter entity (e.g. "Abraham") would match inside the data-word="..." attribute of a longer entity (e.g. "before Abraham was, I AM") that had already been wrapped, producing broken HTML with stray `">` leaking into the visible text. Fix: split the running string into ALREADY-MARKED and PLAIN segments using the existing mark tags as separators, only annotate plain segments. (2) The `tryApplyFilter` LS-rehydration latch in codex-controls.js only fired when corpusId was set, so a page reload with religion-only state (e.g. {religionId: christianity, corpusId: null}) left the wheel unfiltered. Fix: latch now fires on religionId OR corpusId. Bundle rebuilt via scripts/build-forge-bundle.sh. SCRIPTURE_TEXTS 190→196 entries. Gospel of John: 6→12 chapters (1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 11, 14, 15, 17, 19, 20). Gates: node --check ✓, linkcheck 489/621 holds, lint 0 errors, build_data 4747/21757. Cache-bust: _bundle.js → 20260527-john-reload-religion-latch, scripture-texts.js → 20260527-john-showcase-12-chapters. Slot CLOSED.

> **`watcher-claude-religion-wheel-filter-2026-05-27` — FINISHED 2026-05-27 — religion-level wheel filter wired (filtering stages now match the pill cascade).** John caught a real bug after the All-scriptures sentinel fix: the Religion pill changed labels and the Codex picker filtered correctly, but the WHEEL ITSELF didn't respect the religion pick — picking "Mormon (LDS)" or "Christianity" still showed scriptures from all religions on the wheel. Engine-side filter in forge.js rebuildForMode only read `local.codexFamily` (= corpus), so when religion was picked without a corpus, the filter didn't fire and the default SCRIPTURE_IDS view stayed visible. Fix: (1) Exposed `window.SCRIPTURE_RELIGIONS` from codex-controls.js so the engine can resolve religion → corpora → docNodes. (2) Added a religion-level filter branch in forge.js rebuildForMode right after the corpus-level branch: when `local.codexReligion` is set without `local.codexFamily`, the engine collects all docNodes from all corpora in that religion and filters modeNodes to that union set. (3) The religion-pick handler in codex-controls already calls rebuildForMode, so the cascade now actually fires the wheel update. Verified live: pick Christianity → wheel shows only the 65 unique Christian docNodes (Bible 33 + Tewahedo 13 + Kebra Nagast 1 + Reformation 2 + Spanish Mystics 2 + Cathar 2 + Nag Hammadi 17 with overlap-dedup = 65); the Vedic/Buddhist/Mesoamerican/Hermetic/Mesopotamian sectors empty out. Cache-bust: _bundle.js → 20260527-religion-wheel-filter, forge.js → 20260527-religion-level-filter. Gates: node --check ✓, linkcheck 489/621 holds, lint 0 errors. Slot CLOSED.

> **`watcher-claude-codex-pill-5step-2026-05-27` — FINISHED 2026-05-27 — Codex pill restructured from 4-step → 5-step per John's workflow brief.** Was: `Family (= corpus) → Books → Lens → ✠ Read`. Now: `Family (= RELIGION) → Codex (= corpus) → Books → Lens → ✠ Read`. John pointed out that "Family" should be the RELIGION outer step (Christianity / Islam / Judaism / etc.) and a new "Codex" step in the middle picks the specific canon WITHIN that religion (Christianity → Bible / Ethiopic Tewahedo / Kebra Nagast / Reformation / Spanish Mystics / Cathar / Nag Hammadi; Islam → Qurʾān Nöldeke / Qurʾān Manzil / Hadith / Islamic Mystic / Persian Sufi / Shīʿa / Alevi; etc.). Added `SCRIPTURE_RELIGIONS` map in src/js/forge/codex-controls.js grouping the 42 corpora into 22 religions, with `corpusToReligion` reverse-lookup. State shape bumped LS `atlas.codex.v1` → `atlas.codex.v2` (added `religionId`; renamed `familyId` → `corpusId`; v1→v2 migration via reverse-lookup runs on first load). New Religion pill button added before the existing Codex (family) button. New menu builder `buildReligionMenu` lists 22 religions + "All families" sentinel; updated `buildFamilyMenu` to filter codex list by picked religion (or show all-grouped-by-religion if "All families"). > **`opus-2026-05-24-evening-severity-dogma` — IN PROGRESS 2026-05-24 — Lane B. The bottom-right timeline toolbar (LIN/LOG/LOG-R/CMP + DENSITY) shipped in Phase 22-AA was visibly 2-3px taller than the canonical bottom-bar buttons. I attempted three fixes by tweaking inline styles — all failed because inline `Object.assign(el.style, {...})` cannot be pixel-identical to a CSS class (default `box-sizing: content-box` adds the border on top of explicit `height`, and `line-height` defaults differ). John invoked the SEVERITY DOGMA after the third strike. **Now codified in `HOW-WE-WORK.md` §5.7**: three-strike-termination rule for UX work, with explicit clauses against (a) missing the actual problem, (b) duplicating canonical primitives in inline styles, (c) +1px patches instead of fixing the rule. **Handoff at `00_meta/HANDOFF-2026-05-24-evening.md`** documents the bug, the dogma, the queued fixes (552 person stubs, calendar presets, 3-vector dating model), and what the correct toolbar implementation looks like (use `.forge-fxpanel-btn` class, not inline mimicry). Active task = ship the toolbar using the canonical class.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21g-2026-05-21` — BG anchored in WORLD UNITS (fundamental fix) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. After a long iteration through viewport-relative BG sizing schemes (Phases 20F-21F) that each had different cascade-related failure modes, John identified the architectural root cause: "WE SHOULD fundamentally setup world sizes ??????????? exactly to avoid these scenarios". The BG image was the only object in the engine without a world-unit definition — every pie slice, every deity dot, every divider sits in the canonical 1128 wu coordinate space, but the BG was being measured in viewport pixels and gizmo-percentages, which is why each iteration had a different failure mode. **One change**: declared `BG_WORLD_WIDTH = 18000` wu (~16× the wheel's 1128 wu extent) in `src/js/views/forge.js`. `syncBackgroundImage()` now computes `widthPx = BG_WORLD_WIDTH × camera.state.scale` and `heightPx = widthPx / imageAspect`, then positions via `camera.worldToScreen(0, 0)` + canvas-offset → viewport coords + `translate(-50%, -50%)` to centre on the image's own midpoint. CSS only owns the static layer state (fixed positioning, opacity, will-change hints). **Result**: the BG image is now a world object like the wheel. Same coordinate system, same projection, same pan-and-zoom behaviour — by construction, not by JS arithmetic. At gizmo 10% on any viewport, BG covers viewport with ~50% pan margin on each side. At higher zoom, BG grows in lockstep with the wheel. The question "how big is the BG at any zoom on any screen" has exactly one answer now: `18000 × camera.state.scale` pixels. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-21g`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`. Lane B slot OPEN.

CE Christ-Pantokrator; Mathews 1993 *Clash of Gods*); (4) The Logos / Trinity theology (Egyptian Ptah / Memphite Theology → Plato → Philo → John 1 → Nicaea + Chalcedon; Boyarin 2004 + Segal 1977 + Hurtado 2003); (5) The Soul's Exile and Return (Plotinian Neoplatonism + Gnostic substrate → Pauline kenosis + 'in Christ' pattern → Christian mystical tradition). **Three findings**: (a) the inheritances are LAYERED with substrate-depth varying systematically — oldest is Eucharist (3,500 years deep), youngest is Pantokrator iconography (matures into Christianity); (b) Christianity's TWO MAXIMAL-DEIFICATION MOVES (Eucharistic transubstantiation + Chalcedonian *homoousios* Christology) are the consistent distinctive innovations across ALL inheritances — Christianity inherits broadly but innovates by taking the deification claim to its ontological maximum at two specific structural locations; (c) the cross-tradition map makes BOTH inheritance AND innovation visible at once — unlike standard religious-studies scholarship which tends to do EITHER apologetic-mode (minimize the maximal-deification moves) OR polemical-mode (flatten the inheritances), the Codex Atlas's seven-lens wiring produces a *Christianity-without-apology AND Christianity-without-debunking* dual visibility. **README collection navigator** added at `00_meta/MASSIVE-WIN-essays/README.md` — describes all 5 essays, reading orders, methodology discipline, and links into the Phase 7-16 hub-wiring infrastructure. **PHASE-17 ESSAY SERIES COMPLETE**: 5 essays (~1,127 lines total) covering the four densest cross-tradition hubs + the Christianity-focused synthesis. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21ef-2026-05-21` — BG anchor shift: native-size at gizmo 0% (unreachable), 1.9× at gizmo 10% (floor) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. After Phase 21D's `100vw / 100vh + object-fit: cover` approach failed visibly (image rendered smaller than viewport for an undiagnosed CSS-cascade reason), John asked to flip the diagnostic: "make the image NOT fill the background. just retain its original size." That landed in **Phase 21E** (CSS dropped `width / height / object-fit`; IMG renders at its natural 2668 × 2000) — confirmed the DOM / transform path works (image renders huge, way bigger than viewport). Then **Phase 21F** moved the scale anchor: `scale = 1 + 9 × zoomPct`, so `scale = 1.0` (image at native pixel size) lands at gizmo 0% (the unreachable theoretical limit) and `scale = 1.9` at gizmo 10% (the camera floor — image at 1.9× native, always bigger than any normal viewport on every axis). Panning through the floor always reveals more image, never a gap. Growth rate (`10 × scale per 100% zoom`) preserved from the earlier `zoomPct / 0.10` formula. CSS retains `top: 50%; left: 50%; transform-origin: 50% 50%`; JS transform = `translate(-50%, -50%) translate(dx, dy) scale(s)`. The "no gaps" guarantee no longer relies on a `100vw / 100vh` CSS rule that any parent transform could silently rescale — it relies on the IMG's intrinsic dimensions being big enough that any normal viewport sits comfortably inside the rendered image. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-21f`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21d-2026-05-21` — bulletproof BG: CSS `object-fit: cover` at 100vw/100vh + JS transform follows wheel — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John: "i want to fix the BG ? can you do it ? or its impossible to make a BG fit a screen ?" + "RIGHT NOT THERES BLUE GAPS IN THE BG ALL AROUND THE IMAGE IS NOT FITTING THE ENTIRE WORLD AND IT GETS LIMITED TO ITS SIZE IF THE SCREEN IS BIGGER". **Two changes**: (A) **CSS in `src/styles/app.css`** — `.forge-bg-image` is now FUNDAMENTALLY pinned to the viewport: `position: fixed; top: 0; left: 0; width: 100vw; height: 100vh; object-fit: cover; object-position: center center; transform-origin: 50% 50%`. The browser's own `object-fit: cover` engine guarantees the image fills every viewport corner at every viewport size — JS can't introduce a wrong size. (B) **JS in `src/js/views/forge.js`** — `syncBackgroundImage()` reduced to opacity + a CSS transform. Each camera tick computes `scale = max(0.10, zoomPct) / 0.10` (image grows with zoom; floors at fill-coverage when below gizmo 10%) + `(dx, dy) = (wheel-centre projected to viewport) − (viewport centre)` (image translates to follow the wheel through pan). Final write: `bgImage.style.transform = "translate(<dx>px, <dy>px) scale(<scale>)"`. No more width / height / left / top math. `transform-origin: 50% 50%` ensures scale grows from the same point pan tracks. **The promise**: image covers 100% of viewport at every zoom and every viewport size, full stop. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-21c-2026-05-21` — world-anchored BG image (follows the wheel through pan + zoom) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after 21B: "now appears But its not CENTERED with the WHEEL! I CAN zoom out move around and the BG is static! NO, needs to be stuck with the chart". **Two changes in `src/js/views/forge.js` + `src/styles/app.css`**: (A) **CSS** — dropped `inset: 0; width: 100vw; height: 100vh; object-fit: cover` on `.forge-bg-image`. Left only `position: fixed; z-index: 0; opacity: 0` as the static layer; JS now writes `left / top / width / height` each camera tick. (B) **`syncBackgroundImage()`** — restored full world-anchored math from Phase 21A but with the new body-root parent. Each tick: (1) compute `zoomPct = camera.state.scale / fit_scale` (gizmo terms); (2) opacity ramp 0 at 50% → 1 at 10% (unchanged); (3) `sizeZoomPct = max(0.10, zoomPct)` floor; (4) cover-fit box at gizmo 10% from `aspect` (cached from `bgImage._bgAspect` via load-event listener) vs `vpAspect` — picks the branch that matches max viewport axis edge-to-edge + overflows the smaller axis; (5) `scaleFactor = sizeZoomPct / 0.10` × cover box = actual image dims at current zoom; (6) world (0, 0) → canvas screen via `camera.worldToScreen`, then add `canvas.getBoundingClientRect()` offset to land in viewport coords (which `position: fixed` reads). Result: pan moves the BG with the wheel; zoom scales the BG with the wheel; at gizmo 10% the BG fills the larger viewport axis edge-to-edge; below 10% it stays at fill-coverage (size floor matches opacity floor). Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-21c`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20j-2026-05-21` — hulls anchored at world origin (not drifted centroid) + canonical layout radii + BG image size floor at 7% — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after 20I: "zoom out not filling not even the entire screen…. once again not fucking centered all nodes ignore completely the HULLS." **TWO root causes diagnosed via smoke-test** — measured the actual centroid after layout: **the deity-position centroid drifts to (24.18, −39.35) — 46 wu away from world origin**. But the wedges (and divider lines) are angularly anchored to world (0, 0), so the pie slices were being rendered around a point ~60 px off-screen from where the deity cluster actually projects. Also: `max-from-centroid = 572.9` while `max-from-origin = 534.0` — the data-driven outer radius was ~40 wu bigger than the actual rim, pushing the pie arc out into empty space. **Three fixes**: (A) `src/js/engine/graph/hull.js` — `buildFamilyHulls` now accepts an opts shape `{ wedges, rInner, rOuter }`; the hull `center` is hard-anchored at `(0, 0)`; returned `innerRadius` / `outerRadius` prefer the supplied canonical values (lay.rInner = 220, lay.rOuter = 540) over the data-derived max/min-from-centroid. Backwards-compat with the legacy positional wedge-map arg preserved. (B) `src/js/views/forge.js` `rebuildForMode` now passes `{ wedges: lay.wedges, rInner: lay.rInner, rOuter: lay.rOuter }`. Verified via smoke-test: `center: (0.00, 0.00)`, `innerRadius: 220`, `outerRadius: 540`, 34 hulls + 34 dividers — every metric pinned to the canonical wheel geometry. (C) BG image sizing — added `sizeZoomPct = max(0.07, zoomPct)` floor in `syncBackgroundImage()` so the image stays at fill-coverage when the user zooms BELOW the gizmo's 7% (the camera allows down to absolute scale 0.05, which can map to gizmo percentages well under 7% on landscape viewports). Opacity already had the 7% floor; sizing now matches. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20j`. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/hull.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20i-2026-05-21` — zoom-percentage semantics fix (gizmo % is scale/fit, not raw scale) + max-axis cover-fit — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20H: "image is now filling vertically all height but not the widht, the image should fit always the larger window x or y. all still off — WHAT YOU DOING question." **Root cause diagnosis** — the zoom-gizmo bottom-bar button displays `(camera.state.scale / fit_scale) × 100` as a percentage. So "100%" in the gizmo = fit (NOT camera.state.scale = 1.0), "50%" = `fit × 0.5`, "7%" = `fit × 0.07`. My Phase 20F/G/H were comparing RAW `camera.state.scale` against 0.50 / 0.25 / 0.07, which means the same numeric value gives very different zoom levels depending on the current viewport's fit-scale. John's "7% max zoom out" referred to the GIZMO percentage, not raw scale. **Two fixes in `src/js/views/forge.js`**: (1) `syncBackgroundImage()` now computes `zoomPct = camera.state.scale / computeFitScale()` and uses THAT for both the opacity ramp and image sizing. Image side = `(max(vp.w, vp.h) / 0.07) × zoomPct` — at the gizmo's "7%" the image side equals the LARGER viewport axis (fits edge-to-edge along that axis as John specified — "the image should fit always the larger window x or y"; smaller axis overflows + crops). Reverted Phase 20H's diagonal sizing per John's explicit "larger window x or y" directive. (2) The family-label fade in `syncHulls()` now also uses `zoomPct` (was raw `camScale`) — labels fade out from 50% to 25% zoom-gizmo-percentage. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20i`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20h-2026-05-21` — bumped visible disk-fit buffer + true diagonal cover-fit for BG image — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20G: "the image IS NOT filling the screen at max zoom (FILL CROP). Zero changes in the nodes respecting each hull — what you doing?" **Two fixes in `src/js/views/forge.js`**: (1) Pie-slice radial padding bumped from 14 px to **28 px** total — split into `DISK_CONTAIN_PAD = 10` (contains the disk radius — ~6–9 px disk + a margin) and `DISK_BREATHE_PAD = 18` (the visible buffer between disk edge and slice arc). At default fit-zoom (`scale ≈ 0.65`), the visible gap from outermost disk edge to pie-slice outer arc is now ~18 px — reads cleanly as "the dots are INSIDE their slices with breathing room". The 14-px version (Phase 20G) only gave ~6 px gap which was visually imperceptible — exactly what John flagged as "zero changes". (2) `syncBackgroundImage()` now sizes the image to the viewport DIAGONAL at scale 0.07 (`imgSize = hypot(vp.w, vp.h) / 0.07 × camScale`), not `max(vp.w, vp.h)`. The diagonal sizing guarantees that every viewport CORNER is inside the square image — true `object-fit: cover` behaviour at max zoom-out. The old max-side sizing matched only one viewport axis and left the corners dark on rectangular viewports (which is what John saw in the 7%-zoom screenshot). Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20h`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`. Lane B slot OPEN.

I dont see any BG image…" **Two diagnosed root causes + two fixes**: (1) Pie slices were rendered using `data.outerRadius` (= max DISK-CENTRE distance), but disks extend ~9 wu past their centres — so disk edges spilled OUTSIDE the slice arcs. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20d2-2026-05-21` — organic age-radial fan (replaces 20D's rigid bands) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20D shipped: "this is TOO aligned and mechanical, i want the previous feeling but with the constraint like i said. make it open like a fan and the bigger nodes can occupy bigger areas and the clusters should flow organically NOT rigid." **Three changes** to `src/js/engine/layout/radial.js`: (A) **Initial placement now uses VAN DER CORPUT base-2 sequence** for the angular slot (vdc(1)=½, vdc(2)=¼, vdc(3)=¾, vdc(4)=⅛, vdc(5)=⅝, …). Each rank gets a UNIQUE, well-distributed angular slot in (−1, +1), with consecutive ranks landing FAR apart — eliminates the pile-up that age-sorted Vogel/cos(i·φ) produced. (B) **Radius is soft-jittered age-rank** (±16% of annulus span around the age target) so age-equivalent members spread radially instead of forming a ring. (C) **Fan factor multiplies u by `0.45 + 0.55·radNorm`** — angular spread is 45% at the inner rim, 100% at the outer rim, so wedges literally open like a hand-fan. (D) **2D anti-collision relaxation** (8 iterations) — replaces the 20D angular-only push. Each pair within `minDist` (BASE_SIZE 11 + HUB_BONUS 7·degNorm) pushes apart along the 2D line between them; coincident pairs split along a deterministic hash-bearing. A soft age-spring (`SPRING_K = 0.10`) pulls each member's radius back toward its age target after every push, so radius can DRIFT when angular space runs out (essential for tight wedges) without losing the age ordering. Final clamp keeps everything inside `[rIn, rOut]` and inside the fan-constrained wedge bounds. **Verified live**: min pairwise distance per wedge now 10.5–17 wu (was 0–7 wu pre-fix; zero stacked nodes); Vedic outer rim is all ancient (dyaus-pita −4000 BCE, indra/shiva/vritra/aditi −1500), inner rim is all medieval (bhairava 400, waheguru 1500, radha 800, narada undated); Vedic angle range `[-0.106, +0.100]` spans the full wedge; outer-rim spread (~0.20 rad) is 2× inner-rim spread (~0.10 rad) — fan effect visible. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20d-2026-05-21` — radial layout rebuild (age-radial + thematic family order + halved divider width) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20C: "define inner radius + outer radius, deities span those radii closely while keeping organic spread, laid out from oldest to new from outer to inner within their own family. Members sort by `date_earliest` ASC (oldest first, undated sink to inner), then pack into concentric bands inside each wedge. Band capacity = floor(usableArc × meanR / 52) clamped to [1, 6, N] — wide wedges (Vedic 60, Greek 48) get 4-5 per band, narrow ones (Manichaean 4) get 1-2 per band, but every family fills its wedge cleanly from rOuter→rInner. Within a band, members fan evenly across the wedge arc; alternate bands shift half a sub-slot for brick-row stagger (organic, not gridded). Radial position uses the equal-AREA formula `r = sqrt(rIn² + (k+0.5)/K · (rOut² − rIn²))` so band density is visually balanced inner-vs-outer. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20b-2026-05-21` — radial family titles outside the ring + separator lines between adjacent families — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 20: titles were cluttered inside the wheel, asked for them OUTSIDE the outer ring. Also asked for radial separator lines between each family, overshooting the inner + outer radii by 50 px, last 15% on each endpoint fading to 0. **Fix**: extended `buildFamilyHulls` to compute (a) wheel center (mean of all packed positions), (b) inner + outer ring radii (min/max distance from center), (c) per-family centroidAngle via CIRCULAR MEAN (sum unit vectors then atan2 — wrap-safe), (d) dividers[] — angular bisectors between angularly-adjacent families. Return shape changed from `Array` to `{ hulls, center, innerRadius, outerRadius, dividers }`. **Rendering**: SVG overlay now has 4 ordered children — `<defs>` (per-line linearGradients) + `polys` group + `dividers` group + `labels` group — polys paint first, dividers in the middle, labels on top. `syncHulls()` now also measures the ring's SCREEN radius once per frame (via worldToScreen of an edge point) so divider overshoots + label offsets work in screen px regardless of camera scale. **Labels**: position = centerScreen + (ringPxRadius + 28) × (cos a, sin a). Outside the rim everywhere; no more overlap with the node cloud. **Dividers**: from (innerPx − 50) to (outerPx + 50) along bisector angle. Each line uses `stroke="url(#forge-hull-divgrad-N)"` with a per-line `<linearGradient gradientUnits="userSpaceOnUse">` whose 4 stops are 0%(α=0) → 15%(α=0.55) → 85%(α=0.55) → 100%(α=0) — the gradient endpoints are updated each frame to track the line, so the fade rides the line correctly through pan/zoom. **Verified live**: 34 dividers + 34 gradients + 34 labels + 34 polys. Labels rendered: VEDIC, BUDDHIST, CHINESE, EGYPTIAN, AFRICAN, ETRUSCAN, HERMETIC, MYSTERY, GREEK, ROMAN, CELTIC, BALTIC, SLAVIC-FINNIC, CHRISTIAN, GNOSTIC, NEOPLATONIST, MANICHAEAN, MANDAEAN, PRE-ISLAMIC-ARABIAN, ISLAMIC, MESOAMERICAN, ANDEAN, NATIVE-AMERICAN, PACIFIC, SHINTO, MODERN-ESOTERIC, OTHER, CANAANITE, ISRAELITE, ZOROASTRIAN, ARMENIAN, NORSE, JAIN, PRE-CHRISTIAN-MAGYAR. Separator lines visible as thin gold-tinted radial spokes between every adjacent family arc, fading at both ends. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-20b`. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/hull.js` (centroidAngle + dividers + ring metrics), `src/js/views/forge.js` (hull SVG restructure into 4 ordered groups + per-line gradient updates), `src/styles/app.css` (.forge-hull-divider + .forge-hull-label-g). Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-20-2026-05-21` — family hulls + tradition titles overlay — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John recco-confirmed: convex hulls grouped by `family` (34 buckets, much cleaner than the 413 `tradition` strings). New module `src/js/engine/graph/hull.js` exports `buildFamilyHulls(packedNodes, nodesById)` + `convexHull(points)` (Andrew monotone chain) + area-weighted polygon centroid. Per family: gather world positions, run convex hull, compute centroid. Larger families first so smaller hulls layer on top. **Rendering**: new SVG overlay `.forge-hulls-overlay` inside `.forge-stage` between canvas and labels at z=3, pointer-events:none. `rebuildHullElements()` creates one `<g>` per family on mode-switch; `syncHulls()` walks the list each camera change, worldToScreen on every vertex + centroid. **Zoom fade**: opacity 1.0 below 2.0x, linear ramp to 0.0 at 3.0x. Skip per-vertex math when fully faded. **Visual**: hull poly fill 5% family-color + stroke 32% at 1.25px round-joined. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-17-2026-05-21` — hover card position rewrite (anchor-once + rAF-coalesce) + wire Wikipedia thumbnails — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John reported the hover card glitched: it started in one position then jumped to another, and at higher zoom levels it'd appear far from the cursor then "stick." Root cause: positionCard was re-evaluating the quadrant choice on EVERY mousemove, which means near a screen corner the card flipped between quadrants on single-pixel cursor moves — pure flicker. Plus the card was positioned BEFORE the image loaded; when the image arrived height changed but the card stayed at its initial position until the next mousemove. **Phase 17 rewrites the positioning as anchor-once + hysteresis.** Anchor (which of 4 quadrants — top-right / top-left / bottom-right / bottom-left of cursor) is picked ONCE at showFor time via `pickAnchor()` — best-fit quadrant for the current viewport. mousemove then only translates by `(cursor + anchorX*OFFSET, cursor + anchorY*OFFSET)` using CACHED dimensions, zero layout reads. Anchor re-flips ONLY when the cached card would actually overflow at the new cursor position (hysteresis — flicker eliminated). rAF-coalesced via `schedulePosition()` so transform writes are capped at 1/frame. **Image load now triggers a re-measure + reposition** — height change post-load no longer leaves the card stale at the pre-image position. **Wikipedia thumbnails wired**: fetch `_assets/thumbs_cache.json` once at view mount (Wikipedia URL + extract per node id, ~1.5 MB cached); look up `cache[id].src` on hover, set `<img src>` directly. Description falls back to the cache's Wikipedia `extract` (first sentence, capped 180 chars) when YAML doesn't have a `role`/`description`. **OFFSET bumped 22 → 38** so even a max-clamped selected disk (22 × 1.5 ≈ 33 px) never sits under the card. **Window resize** invalidates the anchor → hides the card so the next hover gets a fresh quadrant choice. **Verified live**: page boots clean, thumbs_cache.json HTTP 200 (cache loads), no console errors. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-17`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (wireHoverCard — thumbs cache fetch + measure + pickAnchor + applyTransform + schedulePosition; showFor — wires the cache lookup + extract fallback; mousemove handler reduced to rAF schedule), `index.html` (cache-bust). Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-16-2026-05-21` — hover card viewport clamp + offset + show-on-locked + legend plain language — FINISHED 2026-05-21.**

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-15-2026-05-21` — legend tooltip viewport clamp + hover card data rows — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John feedback after Phase 13/14: legend explainer windows get cropped at the bottom of the screen; the hover card needs real content (description, connections-by-bucket, date, place, religion). **(Legend tooltip clamp)** `showTooltipFor` in `wireLegend` now measures the tooltip's actual height after rendering content and clamps top into `[margin, viewport - height - margin]`. Same on the horizontal axis — if the right side would overflow, it flips to the LEFT of the panel. Default position (right of panel, top-aligned with the row) preserved when there's room. **(Hover card rows)** Added 4 new data sections beneath the existing thumbnail + name + tradition: (1) DESC — picks the first non-empty of `role` / `description` / `brief` / `subtitle` / `domains.join(', ')`. Italic, secondary text color. (2) WIRES — flex row of colored pills, one per non-zero wire bucket. Each pill: 6px dot in the bucket's hex (`local.params.active_color_<bucket>` — single source of truth shared with shader + legend) + count. Walks `local.mode.edges` once per show, O(E) — cheap at 3k edges. Order matches BUCKET_ORDER. (3) META — Date + Place rows in `KEY` (uppercase, 36px fixed width) + `value` layout. Date picks normalized `date_earliest/latest` first, YAML `period-active-earliest/latest` fallback; formats as `2000 BCE – 400 CE` or single year. Place picks `region` / `place-of-origin` / `originating-place` / `location` / `origin`. (4) Tradition row at top is now `tradition || family || religion`. All four sections hide themselves when their data is empty — clean default, no orphan labels. CSS uses the same dark + mono + gold-accent system as the rest of the bottom-bar UI. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-15`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (showTooltipFor + wireHoverCard expanded), `src/styles/app.css` (.forge-hover-card-{tradition,desc,wires,wire,wire-dot,meta,meta-row,meta-k,meta-v}). Lane B slot OPEN.

Secondary: 13 new lenses have zero schema docs (top urgency `20_sacred_architecture` at 116 nodes, `08_places` at 107); 16 ORIGINAL lenses have zero READMEs (asymmetry); 10 of 13 new READMEs still say "26-lens" instead of "29-lens". **(Stub quality, `AUDIT/forge-stubs-quality-audit-2026-05-21.md`)** The "stub" flag is essentially meaningless: 1,835 nodes flagged `status: stub` but only 1 is genuinely empty; 590 are ≥30 lines with rich YAML; 132 carry >5 outgoing wikilinks. Frontmatter integrity is excellent (zero filename↔id mismatches, zero malformed YAML). Narrow defects: 17 places/buildings mis-filed in `04_persons/` by `goblin-sacred-architecture-2-A2` (e.g. `ollantaytambo-town`, `valley-of-the-kings`); 3 in-lens duplicate clusters (`radha`, `persecution-as-legitimation`, `sacred-geometry-cosmic-proportion`); 36 Roman-numeral title-case errors (`Thutmose Iii`); 1 fully-blank `tradition-elamite`. Recommendation: one-shot reclassification (≥30 lines + structured YAML → `status: metadata`) instead of mass rewriting. **(Dead-links + orphans, `AUDIT/forge-deadlinks-orphans-audit-2026-05-21.md`)** Vault is now 4,421 nodes / 44,531 wikilinks (was ~2,660 / ~15,885 in older memory snapshots). Dead-link rate dropped 3.3% → 1.74% — recent stub waves did their job. The dominant integrity issue is now ORPHANS: 341 nodes (7.7%) with zero incoming wikilinks. Orphan concentration: NOT in the new lenses (which shipped at 0% — `21_theology`, `22_practices`, `26_calendars`, `08_places` all clean) but in OLDER lenses — `15_philosophy` 44%, `10_music` 36%, `09_symbols` 25%, `03_deities` and `04_persons` at the highest absolute counts (66 + 81). Cross-tradition bridge analysis surfaced 53 zero-edge pairs between sizeable clusters + anemic bridges (e.g. Gnostic↔Mesopotamian = 2 edges; Egyptian↔Gnostic = 2 — flagged as MASSIVE-WIN investigation candidates). **Combined upshot:** the vault is in better shape than memory suggested — the new-lens expansion was clean. Highest-leverage NEXT WORK (in priority order): (1) NODE_DIRS update across 5 scripts [Lane B-ish, ~20 min]; (2) reclassify the 590 false-stubs to `status: metadata` [Lane A, automated]; (3) promote the top-5 high-in-degree theme spine entries [Lane A, content]; (4) move 17 mis-filed nodes from `04_persons/` to their correct lenses; (5) consolidate 3 duplicate clusters; (6) seed orphan-heavy old lenses (`15_philosophy`, `10_music`, `09_symbols`); (7) investigate the anemic cross-tradition bridges (Gnostic↔Mesopotamian + Egyptian↔Gnostic — these are John's MASSIVE-WIN gold). Three full audit docs total ~1500 lines under `AUDIT/`. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-13-14-2026-05-21` — Phase 13 (LEGEND button + explainer tooltip) + Phase 14 (hover thumbnail card scaffolding) + Phase 12 (wire gradient 100→50) — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. Three things in sequence after Phase 11C confirmation. **(Phase 12)** wire RGB gradient target-end multiplier `0.25 → 0.50` — John felt the target end was too dark; 50% gives a clear directional cue without crushing the target end. Single line in EDGE_SHADER `grad_mult = mix(1.0, 0.50, in.edge_t)`. **(Phase 13)** Wire-color LEGEND button. Same height/style as the zoom-gizmo. Click toggles a panel that expands upward listing the 7 wire buckets (Transmission/Parallel/Association/Kinship/Attestation/Polemic/Fusion) with their hex swatch + name. **Architecture rule baked in**: bucket hex colors come from `PARAM_DEFAULTS.active_color_*` — single source of truth shared with the shader. The legend never duplicates hex values; re-tune the param, the legend updates automatically. Hovering a row pops a secondary explainer tooltip describing what each wire type means and the methodology criteria for using it (e.g. Transmission: "documented contact, dated borrowing, or direct lineage"; Fusion: "syncretic merge — Serapis = Osiris + Apis"). Wrapper element `<div class="forge-legend-wrap">` makes the panel anchor to the button, not the bottom bar. Click-outside closes. **(Phase 14)** Hover thumbnail CARD scaffolding. Single persistent `<div class="forge-hover-card">` appended at mount; never recreated. Behavior: hover a node → 150ms delay → card fades in with `{ thumb_image, node_name, node_type }`. Thumbnails loaded by trying `thumbnails/<id>.{jpg,png,webp}` in order with a per-hover stale-load token (prevents flicker on fast cursor movement). Position updated via `transform: translate3d` on canvas `mousemove` (GPU compositing, no left/top thrash). Hidden when the cursor leaves the canvas OR the node is in lockedSet (lock UI is sufficient there). The setHoverId path was extended with a tiny `local._onHoverChange(id)` hook — fires once per hover transition; defensive try/catch so a card-side error never breaks the hover pipeline. Plus the human-perception lesson saved as memory `feedback_describe_what_human_sees.md` (pinned to MEMORY.md index): describe what the user SEES, not the shader math; believe perception as ground truth; multiple Forge bug-classes wasted sessions because I kept proving alpha=1 while John was reporting visible translucency from an unrelated overlay. **Verified live in preview**: page boots clean (no console errors), legend button toggles open ↔ closed, 7 rows render with correct hexes (transmission rgb(90, 75, 213) etc.), hovering FUSION row reveals tooltip "Syncretic merge…" positioned right of the panel, hover-card DOM exists and is wired. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-13-14`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (legend wrapper in bottombar template + wireLegend + wireHoverCard + setHoverId hook), `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (gradient 0.50), `src/styles/app.css` (legend wrap/btn/panel/row/swatch/tooltip + hover-card styles), `index.html` (cache-bust). Plus memory addition. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-11c-2026-05-21` — Phase 11C: HIDDEN preserved across zoom rebake + label-filter for HIDDEN — FINISHED 2026-05-21.** Lane B. John bug-report after Phase 11: "the hidden nodes appear on zoom back — including labels." Root cause: `recomputeFocus` applied the timeline `state = 2.0` override inline, but `rebakeNodes` (fired by camera.onChange when zoom drifts past the N-aware threshold) ran `computeNodeStates` from `focusedSet` without applying the same override — so out-of-range nodes reverted to `state = 1.0` (FADED) on zoom-back, visibly re-appearing. Labels had the same class of bug: `syncLabels` idle-tier branch computed visibility purely from tier + camScale, with no node-state check, so HIDDEN nodes' labels rendered too. **Fixes**: (a) extracted the timeline-override loop into a shared helper `applyTimelineHiddenOverride(idx, states, focusedSet)`; called from BOTH recomputeFocus and rebakeNodes; (b) `syncLabels` idle-tier branch now filters out nodes whose `nodeTargets[i*2] >= 1.5` (HIDDEN). The check reads from `nodeTargets` (the post-override authoritative buffer), so any future filter that hides a node — search, mode-filter, etc. — drops its label automatically without re-coding the loop. **Verified live**: page boots clean, no console errors. Cache-bust `20260521-rebuild-phase-11c`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (helper extracted + called from rebakeNodes + label filter). **Lesson**: any state computed by `recomputeFocus` that's also re-computed by `rebakeNodes` is a divergence-risk surface. The right pattern is a single helper called from both, never duplicated logic. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-11-2026-05-20` — Phase 11: HIDDEN state + timeline upper-bound = today + year-0 marker + fixed-width date boxes — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. After Phase 10 confirmation ("we got it!"), John handed in 4 polish asks for the timeline scrubber. **(1) HIDDEN state**: out-of-range timeline nodes should not just be FADED, they should disappear. Extended the per-node state float to a 3-step ladder — `0 = IDLE`, `1 = FADED`, `2 = HIDDEN` — with the alpha piecewise: `segA × segB_factor` where segA mixes IDLE→FADED for `state ∈ [0,1]` and segB_factor fades FADED→0 for `state ∈ [1,2]`. Both NODE_SHADER and GLYPH_SHADER fragments updated to the same formula. Fragments discard when `final_a < 0.005`. The fade pipeline animates the state float continuously, so out-of-range nodes smoothly fade out instead of pop-out. Timeline override in `recomputeFocus` now writes `states[i] = 2.0` for non-focused out-of-range nodes. **(2) Upper bound = today's year**: `HIST_HI` constant in `deriveBounds` was `3000` (arbitrary), now `new Date().getFullYear()`. Plus a critical fix: the century-rounding `Math.ceil(hi/100)*100` was bumping `2024 → 2100` AFTER the clamp, defeating it. Added a second clamp post-rounding: `if (hipad > HIST_HI) hipad = HIST_HI;`. Now the timeline tops out at the current year cleanly. **(3) Year-0 reference tick**: new `.forge-scrub-year-zero` element inside the track, positioned by `dateToFrac(0) × 100%` in `refreshUI`. Hidden when bounds don't straddle 0 (defensive). CSS makes it 1px wide × 10px tall, grey-faded at 0.45 opacity, z-index 0 (behind the thumbs). Pure visual cue, doesn't intercept clicks. **(4) Fixed-width date boxes**: `.forge-scrub-box` switched from `min-width: 56px` to `width: 86px` — sized for the longest readout ("15000 BCE" = 9 chars × 7px + 22px padding/border). No more jumping as the user scrubs through values of different string length. **Verified live in preview iframe**: `_forgeDebug.timeline()` returns `{ lo: -15000, hi: 2026, ... }`. The bottom bar shows `15000 BCE | slider | 2026 CE | center_date`. Year-zero element exists in the DOM. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-11b`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (NODE_SHADER + GLYPH_SHADER fragments — piecewise state→alpha + discard), `src/js/views/forge.js` (timeline override → 2.0 for HIDDEN, deriveBounds clamp-to-today + post-round re-clamp, year-zero element in template + refreshUI positioning), `src/styles/app.css` (box fixed-width + year-zero tick style). **John's 4-state node model now fully implemented**: IDLE (full opaque), OVER (same + bigger + stroke), FADED (alpha 0.25), HIDDEN (alpha 0, discarded). Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-10-2026-05-20` — Phase 10: timeline filter no longer overrides explicit focus — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John screenshot of two hovered Mesopotamian deities: Tammuz/Dumuzi rendered properly lit (OVER); Ningirsu rendered FADED with a stroke ring (bug). Same render path, same family color, only the `date_earliest` value in the YAML differs. **Root cause** (line 2342 in forge.js): the timeline scrubber filter loop forced `states[i] = 1.0` on any out-of-range node — INCLUDING nodes the user explicitly hovered or locked. So if a node's `date_earliest` was outside the current scrubber range, hovering it produced `selected=1, state=1` simultaneously — bigger gold-stroked disk at FADED opacity (state_alpha = 0.25). Ningirsu's YAML date was outside the scrubber range; Tammuz's wasn't (or was undated, which the override already skips). **Fix**: skip `focusedSet` members in the timeline override. User's explicit focus beats automatic filter — if you point at a node, you see it. One-line change inside recomputeFocus: `if (focused && focused.has(idx[i])) continue;` before the date check. **Verified**: page boots clean, no console errors. The bug class (state=1 + selected=1 simultaneously) is structurally impossible now: focusedSet ⊇ selectedSet, and any node in focusedSet is skipped by the override → can't be forced to state=1. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-10`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (recomputeFocus timeline override loop). **Lesson**: when two filters can both write to the same state value, they need a priority rule. Default rule: user-explicit (hover/lock) beats automatic (timeline / search filter / etc.). Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-9-2026-05-20` — Phase 9: FADED state is alpha-based (0.25), no color changes — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's correction after Phase 7+8: *"dont want different colors, like i said, THE change of color MAKE it a change of opacity SAME LOGIC."* Plus the 4-state spec: IDLE = 100% opaque + family color + base size; OVER = SAME 100% opaque, just bigger + stroke; FADED = same color at lower opacity (e.g. 0.25); HIDDEN = future. Phase 7's color-darkening of FADED disks (`fill_rgb × dim_mult`) violated the spec — John wants the family color UNCHANGED in all states; only OPACITY varies. **Fix**: switched both NODE_SHADER and GLYPH_SHADER fragments back to alpha-based dim. New fragment math: `state_alpha = mix(1.0, 1 - dim_amount, state); final_a = state_alpha × disk_alpha;` — RGB stays at the family color in IDLE and FADED, only alpha changes. PARAM_DEFAULTS: `dim_amount_nodes: 0.90 → 0.75` and `dim_amount_glyphs: 0.90 → 0.75` so FADED state alpha = 1 - 0.75 = 0.25 per John's spec. Safe to do now because Phase 7 already deleted the discard threshold that previously caught dim disks below 0.15 alpha — alpha-based fade no longer makes disks vanish. **Verified live in preview iframe**: idle screenshot shows the full wheel of 676 deities at full opacity (every family color clear); lock Toyouke shows the focus cluster (Toyouke + 1-hop neighbors) at full opacity and the rest translucent at 0.25 alpha — you can see colored dots faintly across the background. No console errors. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-9`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (NODE_SHADER fragment + GLYPH_SHADER fragment alpha rule), `src/js/views/forge.js` (PARAM_DEFAULTS dim semantics comment + values). **Lesson**: when the user says "same logic" — they mean literally the same mathematical operation. The FADED → OPACITY DROP rule is just `final_a × (1 - dim_amount)`. Color stays color. Alpha is alpha. No mixing the two channels. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-8-2026-05-20` — Phase 8: GLYPH NESTED WITH DISK, WHITE TINT, no independent visibility rules — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's feedback after Phase 7: *"why they are all idle opacity when over and selected? why some don't display the symbol? also check the rules of visibility on zoom scales between the node objects if they match — they shouldn't match, they should be NESTED to each other."* The first two complaints traced to Phase 6B (focus rule hiding glyph on focused/selected) and Phase 6A (screen-size fade creating "missing symbol at this zoom" frames). The third was the architectural insight: **disk and glyph should be NESTED geometry with one visibility rule, not two independent rules**. Phase 8 fix: **DELETED Phase 6A screen-size fade**, **DELETED Phase 6B focus rule**, **DELETED Phase 5C-era per-instance tint coloring of glyph**, **DELETED glyph_opacity uniform** from the alpha math. New rule: glyph paints in WHITE (Apple-icon style, universal contrast on any family color); alpha follows tex.a directly; color dims via the same `dim_mult = mix(1, 1 - dim_g, state)` formula as the disk (so glyph dims with the disk when state=1). Glyph quad size = disk_radius × glyph_scale × selected_size_mult — strictly nested with the disk. The fragment is now ~8 lines: sample atlas, discard if transparent, output `vec4(white × dim_mult × tex.a, tex.a)`. Same vertex z = disk z (Phase 1B invariant preserved). Removed `screen_r` interpolator from VsOut (no longer needed). View struct + uniforms unchanged. **Verified live in preview iframe**: idle screenshot shows the full wheel of 676 deities as solid colored disks (each family clearly visible by hue); lock Toyouke shows the focus cluster + visibly dimmer background (each dim disk darker but still readable). No console errors. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-8`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (GLYPH_SHADER vertex + fragment rewritten + VsOut shrunk), `src/js/views/forge.js` (spec-lock FX section Phase 6A/6B comments replaced with Phase 8). **Lesson**: nested objects need ONE visibility rule, not two. The moment glyph had a `smoothstep(5,10,screen_r)` that disk didn't, frames where one rendered but not the other became inevitable — and confusing to debug because the diff wasn't in either object's own logic, it was in the asymmetry between them. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-7-2026-05-20` — Phase 7: HARD-CORE SIMPLE NODE — strip glow halo + alpha-based dim + discard floor; rebuild around (size, fill_color, stroke) — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's call after Phase 6B: *"WHAT COMPOSITED ALPHA? I been saying I DONT WANT MASKS, I WANT THIS BUILD SOLID BULLETPROOF SIMPLE SHIT."* Followed by the clearer architectural ask: list the existing node states, then strip the rendering to the minimum architecture a human can read at a glance. The bug class chain (glow square-clip → FX5 floor 0.15 → dim disks discarded at 0.15 → flicker during fade transitions) all traced back to ONE thing: **the glow halo is composited via alpha + a discard threshold, and the same threshold was killing every dim disk**. Phase 7 is the architectural reset: **DELETED the glow halo entirely**. **DELETED the discard threshold (0.15)**. **DELETED alpha-based dim** — dim is now COLOR-based (`dim_mult` multiplies RGB, not alpha; disks stay fully opaque, just darker). **DELETED the quad-scale headroom** (`selected_glow.w × 1.5`) — quad is 1.0× always, ONE shape per node. **REPLACED the glow** with a solid stroke ring drawn INSIDE the disk's outer edge in the same SDF fragment (no separate object, no separate alpha annulus, no composite math). **New SELECTED visual rule**: bigger disk (size_mult bumped 1.20 → 1.50) + a gold stroke band at the outer edge (width = `selected_stroke_width` = 0.12 of radius). The fragment shader is now ~12 lines of actual logic. **State table (4 declared, 1 transitional):** IDLE/FOCUSED → full family color at base size, no stroke; SELECTED → full color at 1.5× size with gold stroke ring; DIM → darker family color (RGB × 0.1 at default dim_amount_nodes=0.9) at base size, no stroke; TRANSITION → fill_rgb interpolates smoothly via state float, no pop-out at any threshold because nothing in the fragment is conditional on alpha crossing a value. PARAM_DEFAULTS cleanup: `selected_glow_strength` + `selected_glow_extent` DELETED. `selected_glow_color` RENAMED → `selected_stroke_color`. `selected_size_mult` bumped 1.20 → 1.50. Added `selected_stroke_width: 0.12`. View uniform binary layout unchanged (192 bytes); only the meaning of the trailing SELECTED slot changed from glow → stroke. Spec-lock header in forge.js rewritten with the Phase 7 reasoning. **Verified live in preview iframe**: zero console errors. Idle: 676 disks render solid with concentric-ring deity stencil on top. Lock Toyouke: focused 1-hop cluster shows solid disks; dim background visibly dim (darker, NOT discarded). **Bug class permanently closed**: no more discard threshold = no more disks vanishing during fade. No more glow annulus = no more square-clip class. No more alpha-based dim = no more disappearing dim disks. The node IS a colored circle with simple attributes; the shader reads like prose. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-7`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (NODE_SHADER rewritten + uniform writes + view struct field names), `src/js/views/forge.js` (PARAM_DEFAULTS + drawFrame call + spec-lock FX header), `index.html`. **Lesson**: when iterating doesn't converge after 3+ tries on the same bug class, **rip out the layer**, don't tweak its parameters. The glow halo was 2010-era visual decoration that cost us 5 sessions of cascading fixes. Phase 7 deletes it and the bug family along with it. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-6b-2026-05-20` — Phase 6B: GLYPH FOCUS-RULE — the actual architectural fix for "locked nodes transparent" — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. **John caught Phase 6A wasn't the fix** (*"WHY THEY ARE TRANSPARENT ?????? i keep hearing 'found a bug' BUT its that HARD to CHECK IF THERES ANY CODE PART THAT HIDES OR MAKES IT DISAPPEAR ?"*). Two screenshots showed the actual bug: (a) at mid-zoom, focused/hovered deities render as TORUS shapes — gold halo ring outside, dark/empty inside, with only a tiny concentric-ring deity glyph in the center. The disk between the halo and the glyph icon is gone. (b) Toyouke and other locked disks read as washed pastel because the deity-stencil atlas (a solid filled inner circle at radius 2.2 of 12-unit viewBox + an outer stroke ring at radius 4.6) overpaints the disk center at glyph_opacity × 0.85 ≈ 75% effective alpha. The "transparent inside" perception was the inner-fill of the deity stencil masking the disk's family color with the lightened tint. Phase 6A (screen-size fade) only kicked below 5 px — at the zoom John works at, the glyph was rendering at full opacity over the disk. **Phase 6B (the architectural fix)**: glyph fragment shader gains a focus rule. When focus is ACTIVE (any hover or lock), glyphs are HIDDEN on focused/selected disks (state=0) — those render clean solid + halo — and only show faintly on DIM background nodes (state=1) for at-a-glance type ID. In IDLE (no focus), glyphs paint on every disk at full opacity as before. **Implementation**: JS gates `dimAmountGlyphs` on `hasFocus` (drawFrame line ~1870) — 0 when idle, param value (0.9) when active. Fragment uses `is_active = step(0.01, dim_g); mult = mix(1.0, state * (1 - dim_g), is_active);`. Trace: idle s=0 → mult=1; active s=0 → mult=0 (HIDDEN); active s=1 → mult=0.1 (faint context). **Verified live**: (a) idle screenshot — all 676 deity disks show their concentric-ring deity stencil at full opacity (correct background reading). (b) lock Toyouke screenshot — Toyouke renders as a CLEAN SOLID PINK DISK with gold halo (no glyph wash); 1-hop neighbors Nisaba/Mama Sara/Changing Woman/Demeter all render as solid disks too; background dimmed disks. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-6b`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (GLYPH_SHADER fragment focus rule), `src/js/views/forge.js` (dimAmountGlyphs gated on hasFocus + spec-lock header). **Lesson**: when the user says "X is transparent," verify the disk fragment IS rendering — but ALSO check what OVERPAINTS it. The fragment passing the test doesn't mean the user SEES it — a later opaque overlay can hide it entirely. The `edgesAndNodesOnly()` toggle bisects this in one click. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-6a-2026-05-20` — Phase 6A: GLYPH SCREEN-SIZE FADE (partial fix, superseded by 6B same session) — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. After writing the handoff, kept going at John's request (*"you still have bandwidth to build? if so, go!"*). Diagnosed via debug fragment shader that painted disk-interior RED + glow-annulus GREEN: Toyouke's disk WAS rendering at full opacity at the center (red dot visible). The "transparent" perception was the **deity glyph atlas stencil covering ~85% of the small disk** in a `lightenColor(family_color, 0.55)` pastel tint, washing the disk's true red to pastel pink. Confirmed by toggling `edgesAndNodesOnly(true)` — Toyouke's disk shows as a bright solid red with the glyph pass off. **Fix (architectural, not numeric):** add a screen-size fade in the GLYPH_SHADER. Vertex shader now computes `screen_r = r * abs(view_scale.x) * viewport.x * 0.5` (the apparent on-screen radius in pixels) and passes it to fragment. Fragment multiplies the final alpha by `smoothstep(5.0, 10.0, screen_r)`. Below 5 px the glyph is fully faded out — the disk's family color reads solid and clean at fit-zoom (the overview where John works). Above 10 px (when the user zooms in to inspect a node) the glyph paints at full opacity, exposing the type symbol. Smooth blend in between. Zero per-frame JS work — all computation in GPU. **Verified live in preview iframe**: deities mode boots clean, FRAME 0.2 ms, Toyouke (locked from LS) renders as a solid pink disk + subtle gold halo — the pastel wash is gone. All other deities ALSO read as solid colored disks at fit-zoom. Reverted the diagnostic shader override (RED disk-interior / GREEN glow-annulus) + the temp `_forgeDebug.probeStateSlice` / `probeForceTick` surfaces that were used to confirm fade-pipeline correctness in the headless preview env (rAF is throttled in headless, so nodeStates wasn't converging without explicit ticking — confirmed tickNodeFades works correctly when invoked, ruling out fade-pipeline bugs as the cause). Updated the FX section of the spec-lock header in forge.js with the Phase 6A bullet. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-6a`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (GLYPH_SHADER vertex+fragment), `src/js/views/forge.js` (spec-lock comment), `index.html`. **Lesson saved**: when John reports "X looks transparent," the disk fragment shader is usually doing its job — the bug is upstream in compositing, not the SDF math. Use `edgesAndNodesOnly` to bisect disk-vs-glyph in one toggle before deep-diving the disk math. Lane B slot OPEN.

JUST inspect again and log out clear with description of your plan and current stage with my status and your clues what could be."* Wrote [`00_meta/HANDOFF-2026-05-20-evening.md`](HANDOFF-2026-05-20-evening.md) — covers the full 6-phase rebuild arc + status table (Phases 0–5C shipped across 9 Lane B commits this session; Phase 6 not started), John's current residual (locked Shinto deities like Toyouke-Ōmikami still rendering with transparent-looking disk fill + faint glyph + glow halo), and 7 ranked hypotheses with the specific tests to discriminate them. > **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-5c-2026-05-20` — Phase 5C of the layered Forge rebuild: node + glyph opacity UNIFIED on GPU — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Triggered by John's architectural critique mid-Phase-5B verification: *"This STILL is not a solid build, where the nodes is build with fancy or gimmicks with multiple objects hidding and not higgind — instead of a NODE that just transforms properties in SIMPLE TERMS AVOIDING BUGS"* + "getting a bit slow now outside hovering." Both symptoms traced to the SAME root cause: disk and glyph had two different opacity pipelines — disk opacity uniform-driven in GPU, glyph opacity computed CPU-side per frame in `refreshGlyphAlphas`. Two paths, one notional "node" — they could drift, and the JS loop was the slow-pan source. Fix: move glyph opacity to GPU using the SAME dim formula as the disk. **(View struct extended)** Repurposed the 8th unused `bucket_hot_colors` slot as `glyph_params: vec4<f32>` (xy = glyph_opacity + dim_amount_glyphs). EDGE_SHADER clamps to 0..6 now (only 7 real buckets). UBO size unchanged (192 bytes). **(GLYPH_SHADER fragment redesigned)** Now computes `a = tex.a × in.tint.a × glyph_op × dim_mult` where `dim_mult = mix(1.0, 1.0 - dim_amount_glyphs, in.state)` — exact mirror of NODE_SHADER's disk dim formula. State comes from `nodeStateVbo` (same buffer the disk reads). One opacity logic, one place, drift impossible by construction. **(`refreshGlyphAlphas` DELETED entirely)** The per-frame O(N) CPU loop is gone — it was the only thing setting alpha column values per frame. Now the alpha column is set ONCE to 1.0 at rebuild time and never touched again. The shader does the rest via uniforms + state. **(`local.glyphInstancesDirty` flag REMOVED)** No longer needed — state changes propagate via `nodeStateVbo` which is written every frame anyway. Removed the setters in `camera.onChange`, `recomputeFocus`, `tickNodeFades`, `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer`. Replaced with a simpler `local._glyphRebuildDirty` that's true only when `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer` reallocates (i.e., on mode-switch/rebake) — the static-VBO upload gate, not the per-frame state gate. **(Off-viewport cull removed)** The CPU cull I had was redundant — WebGPU's vertex-clip step culls off-viewport instances for free. The remaining cost (vertex shader running for off-screen instances) is microseconds at any plausible N. **What this fixes**: the visual inconsistency John reported (Toyouke/Ninurta/Mullissu rendering differently despite being same type+similar family) cannot recur — every glyph computes opacity from the same uniforms + the same per-instance state via the same formula. Slow-pan eliminated — `camera.onChange` no longer triggers per-frame JS work for glyphs. **Code reduction**: ~110 lines deleted (refreshGlyphAlphas function + all dirty-flag plumbing + the off-viewport cull math). **Verified live in preview iframe**: deities mode boots clean (676 nodes / 3033 edges / FRAME 0.1 ms / zero console errors); atlas still `{640, 512, 10 mip levels}`; locked Zeus from LS still hydrates + glow halo visible; focused 1-hop neighbors (Apollo, Athena, Hera, Hephaestus, etc.) light up with their glyphs intact; pan/zoom smooth. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-5c`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js` (refreshGlyphAlphas DELETED + dirty-flag plumbing simplified + drawFrame passes glyph uniforms), `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (View struct + GLYPH_SHADER + EDGE_SHADER clamp + drawFrame uniform write), `index.html` (cache-bust), `00_meta/ACTIVE-UX.md`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. **Lesson saved**: `feedback_audit_formulas_need_default_check.md` extended — this is the THIRD audit-recommended numeric threshold that regressed in one session (FX5 discard floor 0.05 vs 0.15; FX2 cull threshold 4 vs 2.55; and now this — though Phase 5C is architectural not numeric, it removes the entire class of CPU/GPU opacity drift). The pattern is consistent: trust empirical observation over audit-prescribed numbers; always check defaults; prefer architectural simplification over numeric tweaks when correctness keeps slipping.

> **`opus-forge-fx5-floor-fix-2026-05-20` — restore the 0.15 discard floor so the glow square-clip class stays closed — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Mid-session catch by John: the FX5 derived-discard formula (`0.05 + max(0, strength-1.0)*0.1`) assumed strength≥1.0 baseline; PARAM_DEFAULTS has selected_glow_strength=0.50, so the formula gave 0.05 — way LOWER than the empirically-proven 0.15. The whole bug class Phase 4B FX5 was meant to permanently close (square-clip artifact) returned immediately on Mastema, Mullissu, Fujin/Raijin. Fix: `0.15 + max(0.0, strength - 0.5) * 0.20` — keeps 0.15 floor at default, scales UP at higher strength (not below). At strength=0.5 → 0.15 (proven safe); at strength=1.0 → 0.25; at strength=2.0 → 0.45. Lesson saved as memory `feedback_audit_formulas_need_default_check.md`: goblin-proposed derivations assume a baseline; if the result at current PARAM_DEFAULTS is worse than the pre-fix constant, it's a regression in disguise. Verified live: Mullissu locked at 593% zoom — glow halo circular, no square mask. Cache-bust `20260520-rebuild-phase-4b-fx5fix`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `index.html`.

> **`opus-forge-rebuild-phase-1b-2026-05-20` — Phase 1B of the layered Forge rebuild: NODE atom locked — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Implementation batch for the NODE atom of the Forge view per [`AUDIT/forge-rebuild-1A-node-atom-2026-05-20.md`](../AUDIT/forge-rebuild-1A-node-atom-2026-05-20.md). All 5 required items + N6 recommended + N7/N8 polish shipped: **(N1) Hit-test spatial grid** — uniform grid over `local.mode.worldExtent` with `cellSize = 2 × maxRadius` built alongside `hitNodes` in `rebuildForMode` + `rebakeNodes`. `hitTestAt` now queries a 3×3 cell neighborhood (~2-10 candidates) instead of iterating all N. Verified live: at 676 deities the grid is 10×10 cells, 57 occupied, max bucket 37, avg 11.86 per non-empty. Hit-test correctness verified by world→screen round-trip on hitNode #100 ("saklas") — `hitTestAt` returns the same id. **(N2) `nodeInstancesDirty` flag** — `local.nodeInstancesDirty` defaults `true` (so first frame writes), reset to `false` after each `drawFrame`, set back to `true` by every pack site (`rebuildForMode`, `rebakeNodes`). Renderer's `webgpu.js` gates `nodeInstanceVbo` `writeBuffer` on `frame.nodeInstancesDirty || r.grew`. Verified: at 676 deities idle, `countNodeVboWrites()` shows 1 write at mount and stays at 1 for 500ms of idle (delta=0). At 10k that's ~106 MB/s saved GPU bandwidth. State VBO write stays unconditional (fades animate per frame). **(N3) `owned[]` destroy pattern** — `webgpu.js create()` declares `const owned = []; own(o) / disown(o)`. Every eager `device.createBuffer` / `createTexture` wrapped with `own()`. Lazy paths (`ensureBuffer`, `ensureDepthTex`, `setGlyphAtlas`) `disown(old) + own(new)` on grow / replace. `destroy()` iterates `owned[]` then `device.destroy()`. Closes T2.6 (atlasTex / glyphInstanceVbo / glyphUvUbo silent leak). Verified live: `ownedCount()` reports 12 active GPU resources at deities mount. **(N4) `packedAtScale` post-fit fix** — explicit `local.packedAtScale = camera.state.scale;` immediately after `packNodes` in `rebuildForMode`. Was previously only set in `rebakeNodes` (line 2135), saved at runtime only by the `||` fallback in `camera.onChange`. Verified: `dumpPackedAtScale()` returns `{packedAtScale: 0.05, currentScale: 0.05, ratio: 1.0000}`. Removes a latent bug that would reappear the moment anyone "cleans up" the fallback. **(N5) CROSS-PIPELINE INVARIANT comment** at `webgpu.js:1082` (the `nodeStateVbo` write site) — large boxed comment documenting that the glyph pass reads this same buffer as its third vertex buffer, and the contract: allocate-before-glyph-bind, write-before-glyph-pass, length ≥ max(node, glyph) × 8. Phase 4 will mirror with a read-site comment in the glyph pass. Closes T2.8 / 05-C1. **(NODE spec lock header block)** at top of forge.js above `PARAM_DEFAULTS` — 30-line block documenting tier classification, tier radii, screen-px clamp, state channel, selected channel, depth z, GPU layout, lifecycle invariant + `rebuildForMode` ORDER (fit → packNodes → packedAtScale write → buildHitGrid → state buffer allocation). A fresh agent reading forge.js now sees the contract before the code. **(N6 recommended) Tier classifier dedup** — `packNodes` now returns `tierFor` as a fourth field. Callers reuse instead of rebuilding. Eliminates 2 redundant O(N log N) sorts per mode rebuild + the drift risk if classifier semantics change.
