> **`opus-forge-gpu-glyphs-depth-fix-plus-handoff` — depth-direction bug fix + comprehensive handoff doc for fresh agents — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's report after `286308b`: "better but not there yet, lots of nodes hover without the symbol just a bigger circle, log out for fresh agent." Root cause caught: the per-instance z values in `286308b` were BACKWARDS for the depth test. In WebGPU clip space, smaller z = closer to camera = wins less-equal depth test. Disk z values: selected=0.0, focused=0.3, dim=0.6. Glyph z values shipped at 0.01/0.10/0.50 — LARGER than parent disks → depth test rejected the glyph for selected nodes (0.01 > 0.0 → glyph hidden behind its own larger disk). Bug visible across MANY nodes because every hover briefly makes a node selected (selectedSet = lockedSet ∪ {hoverId}), so each hover registered the "bigger disk no glyph" flash. **Fix**: glyph z = parent disk z exactly. Glyphs draw AFTER disks so equal-z passes less-equal test, glyph wins via draw order. For overlapping instances at different z, depth test correctly hides dim glyph behind focused/selected disks — exactly what the old occlusion-zone hack approximated, now native. **Plus comprehensive handoff doc** at `AUDIT/forge-glyph-migration-handoff-2026-05-20.md` for fresh agents picking up Forge work. Covers: the 5 live GPU pipeline components, the 3 obsolete DOM paths (do-not-resurrect list), per-instance data layout, the full iteration log of what we tried + what broke + what worked, things to verify + likely-future-bugs, what's NOT yet done (glyph_tint not wired, icon overrides ignored, alpha-refresh unconditional, atlas at 64px not 128px), file map, debug API examples. Plus three lessons in the doc: (1) don't defer architectural fixes 5 times for "small fixes"; (2) don't trust memory of clip-space conventions (this commit fixes my flipped z direction); (3) don't conflate "fade broken" with "rendering broken" (most fade reports were downstream of DOM thrash). Cache-bust `20260520-gpu-glyphs-depth-fix`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (shader z mapping), `AUDIT/forge-glyph-migration-handoff-2026-05-20.md` (new), `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-gpu-glyphs-cleanup-v2` — three audit-fixes on top of the GPU glyph migration: glyph_scale, selected size_mult, per-instance z-layering — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's first-look feedback after the architectural fix (`1226559`): "substantially better!" but flagged three real visual regressions: (1) glyph sizes felt different from before — they were, the DOM glyph applied a `glyph_scale` multiplier (~0.85) that the GPU pack didn't, so GPU glyphs were ~18% bigger; (2) selected node's glyph appeared missing — selected disk grows by `selected_size_mult` (default ~1.5x) but the glyph stayed at base radius, so the larger disk covered it; (3) overlapping glyphs in dense areas looked chaotic — all GPU glyphs rendered at fixed z=0.02 so last-drawn won, no layering. Fixes shipped: **(a) glyph_scale applied** in `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer` — multiplies the packed radius by `local.params.glyph_scale`, matching prior DOM sizing. **(b) Per-instance (state, selected) bound** to the glyph pipeline as a third vertex buffer (`shaderLocation: 3, float32x2`) — REUSES the existing `nodeStateVbo` (1:1 with nodes, same order) so zero duplicate data, just an additional `setVertexBuffer(2, nodeStateVbo)` call in the glyph pass. **(c) Shader applies `selected_size_mult`** to the quad radius via the new instance attr — selected disk + glyph grow together. **(d) Per-instance z** computed in vertex shader from state/selected: selected anchor glyph at z=0.01 (in front of selected disks at 0.0), focused at z=0.10 (in front of focused disks at 0.3), dim at z=0.50 (in front of dim disks at 0.6). Overlapping glyphs now sort cleanly via depth test instead of last-draw-wins. Plus answered John's "is the fade causing slowness" question: no, per `frame: 0.1ms` measurement in the dump; the perceived slowness pre-GPU-migration was the 663-DOM-spans-rewritten-per-frame cliff which is now gone. The new GPU pipeline's per-frame alpha refresh + 21KB GPU buffer write is sub-ms even at full rate. Cache-bust `20260520-gpu-glyphs-cleanup`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (glyph shader + pipeline buffer binding + draw pass), `src/js/views/forge.js` (rebuild applies glyph_scale, refresh simplified), `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-glyphs-into-webgpu` — the architectural fix John kept telling me to stop deferring; glyphs moved from DOM (663 SVG spans) into the WebGPU canvas via a texture-atlas instanced quad pass — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John called out the circling pattern: *"WHY havent we done that , and we're here circling?"* He was right — every prior commit (occlusion-zone hack, label-pre-create, fade-aware rebakes, hover-coalesce, glyph culling) was a workaround for the DOM/canvas seam that this commit eliminates at the source. **What shipped:** (1) Atlas rasterizer in `src/js/engine/graph/glyph.js` — `buildAtlas(cellPx)` async, rasterizes all 17 type-glyph SVGs into a single Canvas2D image (4×5 grid at 64px cells) with currentColor → white substitution, returns canvas + per-type UV rects + typeToIdx Map. (2) New GLYPH_SHADER in WGSL — instanced textured quads, per-instance `(pos.xy, r, glyphIdx)` + `(tintRGB, alpha)`, samples atlas via uniform UV rects, multiplies by per-instance tint, z=0.02 (in front of all disk depth layers). (3) Renderer wires: shader module + UV uniform buffer + atlas texture (1×1 dummy until upload) + bilinear sampler + new bind-group layout (4 bindings) + new pipeline + per-frame glyph instance VBO + `setGlyphAtlas(canvas, uvRects)` method + glyph render pass at the end of `drawFrame`. (4) View layer: `buildAtlas(64)` fires async at engine boot → `setGlyphAtlas` upload; `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer()` at every `rebuildForMode` packs per-node `[pos.x, pos.y, r, idx, tint.r, tint.g, tint.b, baseAlpha]`; `refreshGlyphAlphas()` per frame reads `local.nodeStates` and updates the alpha column so glyphs fade with focus via the existing animation pipeline. (5) DOM glyph machinery FULLY retired — `glyphOverlay` div, `local.glyphEls` Array, `local.glyphFamilyColor` Map, `syncGlyphPositions` function, `syncGlyphFocus` function (with occlusion-zone hack), `rebakeGlyphsForMode` DOM loop, `.forge-glyph` + `.forge-glyphs-overlay` CSS rules all deleted or no-op'd. setParam routes glyph_opacity / glyph_scale / glyph_tint to `rebuildGlyphInstanceBuffer + drawFrame`. **Verified live**: 0 DOM glyphs in the page after reload, atlas uploaded, GPU pass renders at frame time ~0.1ms (vs prior DOM stall when zoomed out), no console errors. **The whole arc of fixes** documented in `AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md` is now obsolete in the way it matters — there is no DOM/canvas seam to work around, no per-frame ~160k DOM writes to cull, no glyph occlusion zone to hack. Depth pipeline handles everything natively. Cache-bust `20260520-gpu-glyphs-only`. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/glyph.js`, `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-glyph-cull` — viewport + min-size culling on glyphs eliminates the zoom-out perf cliff John discovered — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John's clinching observation: *"IF I zoom REALLY close, it animates well! this is a memory thing most likely."* Pinpointed the perf cliff exactly. Root cause: `syncGlyphPositions` runs every drawFrame (60Hz) and iterates ALL 663 glyphs (for deities mode), setting 4 style properties per glyph (left / top / width / height) = ~160k DOM writes per second. When zoomed IN, off-screen glyphs are composite-clipped by browser → small paint work. When zoomed OUT, ALL 663 SVG glyphs are simultaneously in viewport → browser paints all of them every frame → memory pressure → JS thread can't keep up with rAF → fade stutters. Two cheap culls shipped: **(1) Min-size cull** — glyphs computing to < 4px on screen get `display: none` (too small to convey info). At fit-to-extent in deities mode, 100% of glyphs are culled → zero glyph paint work. **(2) Viewport cull** — glyphs whose screen bbox falls outside `[0, vp.w] × [0, vp.h]` get `display: none`. At deep zoom, only the handful near cursor render. Both use `display: none` (cheaper than `visibility: hidden` because browser skips paint entirely) + state-cache check (only set display when state actually changes — avoids redundant DOM writes). Animation pipeline log doc (`AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md`) updated with discovery + fix as section #7. Cache-bust `20260520-glyph-cull`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md`, `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-hover-coalesce-plus-pipeline-log` — coalesce hover via rAF + drop redundant drawFrame + hoist nodesById + comprehensive animation-pipeline log for future agents — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John flagged IDLE-vs-LOCKED hover asymmetry persists after the label-DOM-precreation fix, called it "architectural flaw, log everything for fresh agents". Three remaining audit recommendations shipped + comprehensive log doc. **(1) Coalesce `setHoverId` via rAF** — was firing `recomputeFocus` synchronously on every pointermove that crossed a node boundary (~120Hz on John's Mac, 120 full recomputes/sec, JS thread can't keep up with rAF, animation stutters). Now schedules ONE `recomputeFocus` per rAF max, light sync updates (cursor class + status text) stay immediate so cursor feedback is responsive. **(2) Drop redundant trailing `drawFrame()` in `recomputeFocus`** — `startAnimLoop` schedules `animTick` which calls `drawFrame` on first iteration, the extra `drawFrame` was one redundant GPU submit per call. **(3) Hoist `nodesById` Map to `local.mode.nodesById`** — built once in `rebuildForMode`, reused by scrubber filter in `recomputeFocus` (was allocating a fresh Map every hover). **Plus** `AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md` — a comprehensive living doc covering: the full architecture (buffers, flow, invariants), every animation-related commit's iteration history (what we tried, what broke, what worked, why), things flagged but not yet fixed, things to NOT do, file map for fresh agents, glossary, debug-via-dumpBugState howto. Future agents touching the animation pipeline MUST read this first. **Remaining architectural note** (documented in the log): IDLE-hover inherently has a magnitude difference vs LOCKED-hover — IDLE→hover flips ~600 nodeStates values (mass dim), LOCKED→hover-change flips ~5-10. The fade animates equally in both, but IDLE looks "busier" because more changes. This is a UX design call (less aggressive dim on IDLE-hover?) rather than a bug. Cache-bust `20260520-hover-coalesce`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `AUDIT/forge-animation-pipeline-2026-05-20.md` (new), `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-audit-fix-fade-flow-restored` — Plan-agent-audited proper fix for IDLE-hover lag + click-feels-buffered + LOCKED-fade-broken regression — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. John flagged that the previous commit (`536f9cb`) broke LOCKED-hover smoothness AND made clicks feel "stuck" — the pre-warm approach was treating the wrong symptom. Dispatched a Plan agent to audit the animation pipeline; it surfaced 5 real bugs ranked by impact. Three highest-priority fixes shipped here. **(1) ROOT CAUSE of IDLE-hover slowness: label DOM materialization, not the fade pipeline.** First hover from settled IDLE created ~47 label divs on the spot (`appendChild` × 47 + a batched reflow) → render-thread stall for 1-2 frames → swallowed the fade visually. LOCKED-hover felt smooth because labels for the locked node ALREADY existed in DOM. Fix: pre-create label DOM for every node in the mode at `rebuildForMode` time (single batched DocumentFragment append for the whole mode). Labels start at opacity:0 (CSS default, no `data-visible`), zero perf cost until shown. First hover is now pure attribute flip — no DOM allocation, no reflow. Verified: 676 labels pre-created at mount in deities mode, 0 visible initially. **(2) `rebakeNodes` was bypassing the fade pipeline** — wholesale-replaced `local.nodeStates` with a fresh `interleavePairs()` result, killing any in-flight node fade by snapping current to target. So zoom drifts or dev-panel slider tweaks during a hover-transition caused the disk fade to JUMP instead of continuing. Mirrored the fade-aware pattern that `rebakeEdges` already used: update `nodeTargets` in place, only resize `nodeStates` when length actually changed. **(3) Pre-warm removed entirely.** With (1) and (2) shipped, the original IDLE-hover lag is gone at its source. Pre-warm was treating the symptom (delayed visual response) when the real cause was the DOM stall. Removed cleanly — fade animation now flows uninterrupted in both IDLE and LOCKED modes. **Audit also surfaced (deferred to follow-ups):** `recomputeFocus` not coalesced — pointermove + pointerup fire it back-to-back in <16ms; scrubber filter rebuilds `nodesById` per call (cheap-ish at 676 nodes, hurts at 10k); redundant trailing `drawFrame()` in `recomputeFocus` (one extra frame per call); `09_symbols/`/`06_themes/` queued renames still pending. Cache-bust `20260520-fade-flow-restored`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN. The audit doc is at `AUDIT/lens-expansion-audit-2026-05-19.md`... wait, that's the lens one — the animation audit lives in the conversation transcript, summarized in this entry.

> **`opus-forge-prewarm-fade-plus-scrubber-v2-plus-glow-bump` — pre-warm fade fixes IDLE-hover delay + 4-box scrubber + filter wiring + glow-clip threshold bump — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Five fixes. **(1) Pre-warm fade animation** — the real root cause of John's persistent IDLE-hover-slow report. The previous reflow-batch fix helped DOM perf but missed the actual issue: `drawFrame` at the end of `recomputeFocus` uses the CURRENT state buffer (pre-animation). animTick advances on the next rAF (~16ms later). For LOCKED-hover the state delta is tiny (most values barely change) so the gap is invisible. For IDLE→hover hundreds of node states flip from 0 to 1 (dim mass change) — the 16ms of "nothing happened" before animation begins reads as a delayed pop, not a smooth fade. Fix: after computing targets but before the first drawFrame, pre-advance both `local.nodeStates` and `local.edgeStates` ~30% toward target. The very first frame shows visible progress, the remaining ~70% completes via tickNodeFades / tickEdgeFades over the next ~7 frames. Hover response is now immediate. **(2) Glow-clip threshold bump** — John flagged Vairocana (and earlier Raijin) still showing the square outline despite commit `e52b3ea`. Root cause: when `selected_glow_strength` is high enough, glow alpha just inside the smoothstep upper bound is above the 0.08 discard threshold → writes depth at z=0.0 → adjacent disk fragments at z=0.3/0.6 fail less-equal test there → square-clip outline reappears. Bumped discard 0.08 → 0.15 in the WGSL node fragment shader. Combined with the existing 1.5× quad headroom, the entire glow tail that was depth-blocking neighbors is now discarded. Trade-off: very-outer glow pixels (alpha < 0.15) don't render — barely visible, far better than the square artifact. **(3) Search bar halved** per John's spec — was `flex: 1 1 320px; max-width: 360px;`, now `flex: 0 1 160px; max-width: 180px;`. **(4) Scrubber redesigned to 4 separate boxes** per John's spec: IN value box | slider | OUT value box | PRESENT value box. All four match the height of zoom-gizmo + search input. Boxes use same dark + mono + gold-accent styling; PRESENT box gets gold-tinted text to distinguish as playhead readout. Drag any of the 3 slider thumbs (IN/OUT/CENTER) and the relevant box updates live. **(5) Scrubber filter wired** — when user narrows IN/OUT (i.e., narrower than the full bounds), `recomputeFocus` now applies a date-range overlap check per node: if the node's existence period `[date_earliest..date_latest]` doesn't overlap `[inDate..outDate]`, its state target snaps to 1.0 (dim). Animates via the existing fade pipeline. Undated nodes stay visible. Probe-verified: dragging IN to 465 BCE correctly flagged 123 deities as out-of-range. **Plus dumpBugState extended** with `timeline`, `nodeTargets`, and `state.other` count fields for future fade-debug. Cache-bust `20260520-scrubber-debug-v2`. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html`, `00_meta/STATUS.md`. Lane B slot OPEN. **Next natural targets**: ship Hulls per the integration plan (audit doc `forge-hulls-integration-plan-2026-05-20.md`); full motif-anchor authorship; goblins for the 7 still-empty 2026-05-18 lenses.

> **`opus-forge-timeline-scrubber-plus-label-reflow-fix` — bottom-bar timeline scrubber UI + label-reflow perf fix + Hulls integration plan — FINISHED 2026-05-20.** Lane B. Three deliveries. **(1) Timeline scrubber** in the bottom bar next to the search input. Three handles — IN (lower bound) + CENTER (playhead) + OUT (upper bound). 180px wide (half the search bar). Same dark + mono + gold-accent styling as zoom-gizmo / search. Range automatically derived from the current mode's nodes: scans every `date_earliest` / `date_latest` (plus YAML-raw fallbacks) and clamps to a sane archaeology window `[-15000, 3000]` so cosmogonic-motif nodes with `date_earliest = -1e9` don't squash the human-history span to a hairline. State on `local.timeline = {lo, hi, inDate, outDate, centerDate}`; readout shows `<in> · <center> · <out>` in BCE/CE format. Drag wired via track pointer-capture + per-handle data attribute. v1 ships the UI + range + readout + drag; filter wiring (dim out-of-range nodes via a separate per-instance attribute) is the natural next batch. **(2) Label-reflow perf fix** for John's "IDLE-hover slow + no fade in/out" report. Root cause: `syncLabels` previously called `el.getBoundingClientRect()` PER new label to force the layout-reflow that arms the CSS opacity transition. Hovering from idle creates ~47 new labels at once → 47 synchronous layouts per pointermove → layout thrash → fade animation stutters → perceived as "no animation, slow hovering". Fix: BATCH the reflow — collect all new labels, force ONE reflow via `document.body.offsetHeight`, then flip `data-visible` on all of them. Same fade-in guarantee, ~N× faster. The LOCKED-state hover felt smooth because lock-mode hovers reuse existing labels (no new DOM creation, no reflow needed). **(3) Hulls integration plan** at `AUDIT/forge-hulls-integration-plan-2026-05-20.md` per John's *"prepare to add the Hulls section, we should incorporate that, its the last big piece of the puzzle for the charts to have that styled decided"*. Plan covers: SVG-overlay approach (mirrors Pantheon V2's annular-wedge pattern), z-stack placement, 7-step implementation sequence (geometry helper → packer → SVG overlay → camera viewBox sync → focus/dim state → dev panel params → optional labels), rejected alternatives (WebGPU shader, Canvas2D), estimated ~2hr Lane B batch when greenlit. Also flagged (deferred): John noted some nodes (e.g., Raijin) still show the glow-clip square outline despite commit `e52b3ea` — likely a glow-strength × discard-threshold interaction; needs targeted shader-side investigation in a follow-up. Cache-bust `20260520-timeline-scrubber-v3` (16 occurrences). Two compounding bugs: (a) `syncLabels` removed `data-visible` from every label then re-added it on visible ones, causing existing labels to flicker through interrupted CSS transitions (opacity 1→0 then 0→1 stacking) — the "VERY slow" perception was transitions never completing. Fix: diff-based update — only toggle the attribute on labels whose membership changed. (b) Newly-created labels skipped the fade-in because the browser's first-paint optimization saw the element's final style before layout; the CSS transition never fired. Fix: force a synchronous reflow via `el.getBoundingClientRect()` between DOM insert and `data-visible` flip. Also dropped fade duration 0.25s → 0.15s (snappier baseline; tunable from `FADE_DURATION` const in forge.js + the matching CSS transition values). Cache-bust `20260519-fade-snappy` (16 occurrences). When passed, each wire's source/target endpoint gets offset to the disk perimeter along the source→target unit vector (×0.92 so just inside the disk's AA halo). Different wires out of a hub now fan from different points on its circumference instead of bundling at one spot. View layer builds the radii map from `nodePack.data[i * NODE_FLOATS + 2]` after `packNodes` runs, passes it in `rebuildForMode` + `rebakeEdges`. Cascade fix: `rebakeNodes` (camera-zoom-aware re-pack) now also calls `rebakeEdges` at the end so edges follow when radii change with zoom. **(3) Node fade animation** — same pattern as the edge fade. `local.nodeStates` is now the live-animating buffer; new `local.nodeTargets` holds the snap-to; new `tickNodeFades(dt)` advances toward target at `FADE_DURATION = 0.25s` (matched to edges). Animates both axes (dim + selected) so focus-dim AND selected-glow ease in/out together. Wired into `animTick` alongside `tickEdgeFades`. **(4) Label fade** — `.forge-label` rule swapped `display:none` → `opacity:0` + `transition: opacity 0.25s ease-out`. JS sets `data-visible="1"` attribute; CSS targets it. Labels leaving the focus set fade out instead of vanishing. `syncLabelPositions` skip rule updated to `!el.hasAttribute('data-visible')`. `.forge-glyph` also got `transition: opacity 0.25s ease-out` so the occlusion fade and dim-state changes ease cleanly. **Plus the portable core sibling-product handoff filed** at `00_meta/the portable core-HANDOFF-2026-05-19.md` per its own instructions (not urgent — trigger = design lock + protection-phase request, none active). > **`opus-forge-glyph-occlusion` — selected-node halo no longer cluttered by neighbor glyphs — FINISHED 2026-05-19.** John flagged a real architectural seam after `e52b3ea`: "the symbols and CIRCLES of each node are independent and the SYMBOLS (not the CIRCLE) are going over highlighted nodes' circles — no hierarchy coherent." Root cause: glyphs are DOM SVG spans (`.forge-glyph` z-index 4 in `.forge-glyphs-overlay`) positioned over the WebGPU canvas. Canvas-side disks z-layer correctly (selected z=0 wins via depth test over neighbor z=0.3/0.6), but DOM glyphs sit on top of the entire canvas with no awareness of which canvas-side disk they geometrically overlap. So a focused-neighbor's glyph visually paints over the selected node's glow halo — breaking the visual hierarchy. **Surgical fix shipped**: `syncGlyphFocus` computes an OCCLUSION ZONE (screen circle = `n.r * camera.scale * selected_glow_extent`) around each selected node; any non-selected glyph whose screen center falls inside any zone gets `opacity: 0`. Selected halo stays clean. Wired `syncGlyphFocus` into `camera.onChange` so occlusion updates live during pan/zoom (was only called on hover/lock change before). Performance: 663 glyphs × 1-3 selected zones = sub-millisecond. **Deferred (architectural)**: move glyphs into the WebGPU canvas via a texture atlas of the 17 glyph SVGs + new per-instance shader pass with same z-layering as disks. Would fully unify the depth pipeline (no DOM/canvas seam), but it's a ~2hr standalone batch. Cache-bust `20260519-glyph-occlusion` (16 occurrences). Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `index.html`. Note: Lane A `goblin-sacred-architecture-1` continues — 15 new sacred-architecture nodes now staged (added Hagia Sophia + Pantheon Rome + Parthenon + Solomon's Temple 1st/2nd + Ziggurat of Ur to the previous 9). Staged only Lane B files; their content + slot-claim untouched. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-glow-glyph-fix` — selected-node glow loses its square-clip, glyphs z-order by state — FINISHED 2026-05-19.** Two follow-up polish items John flagged after `03e1589` shipped, deferred from that commit so the resize-snap-fix could land surgical. **(1) Selected-node glow no longer shows a square clipping outline.** Root cause: the WGSL node vertex shader had `quad_scale = v.selected_glow.w`, so the QUAD's axis-aligned edges sat exactly where the glow's smoothstep falloff ended (`smoothstep(1.0, glow_outer, dist)`). Pixels just inside that boundary had a tiny `glow_a` value above the 0.04 discard threshold and WROTE depth at z=0 (selected layer). Adjacent disk quads at z=0.3/0.6 then failed the depth test there, leaving a visible square-shaped "bite" out of the background. Fix: `quad_scale = v.selected_glow.w * 1.5` — the quad now extends 1.5× past the smoothstep upper bound. Outer pixels return `glow_fade = 0`, `final_a < discard_threshold`, no depth write, adjacent disks paint through. Discard threshold also bumped 0.04 → 0.08 for clean falloff. Result: glow is a perfectly circular falloff with no visible quad geometry. **(2) Glyph z-order is now state-driven.** Each `.forge-glyph` DOM span gets `z-index: 3 / 2 / 1` based on `selectedSet` / `focusedSet` / dim in `syncGlyphFocus`. Was DOM-append-order before, which meant a selected node's glyph could be visually buried under whatever was appended later — visible when neighbors overlap. Cache-bust `20260519-glow-glyph-fix` (16 occurrences). Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `index.html`. Note: Lane A `goblin-sacred-architecture-1` continues — staged only Lane B files; their 9 new sacred-architecture nodes (Göbekli Tepe, Çatalhöyük, Khufu, Djoser, Karnak, Tomar Convent, Temple Church London, Holy Sepulchre, Dome of the Rock) and ACTIVE-CONTENT.md slot claim left for their own commit. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-resize-snap-fix` — root cause of "wheel snaps to center ~1s after window resize" found + pan bounds added — FINISHED 2026-05-19.** Despite removing `fitToExtent` from forge's own `resizeAndFit` (commit `4769653`), John still saw the wheel snap to fit-and-centered after resize. **Root cause**: `src/js/app.js:10267` has a global window.resize handler that debounces 200ms then calls `setView(STATE.view)`. For Forge that means a FULL TEARDOWN: `VIEWS.forge.render()` removes the pane, destroys the engine, mounts a fresh one, which re-runs `setup()` → `resizeAndFit(true)` → fitToExtent. Forge has its own ResizeObserver that handles canvas resize without a re-mount; the global handler was meant for views (timeline etc.) that read clientWidth at mount. Fix in 2 surgical edits: (1) `app.js` window.resize handler now short-circuits if `STATE.view === 'forge'`. (2) Added camera **pan bounds** so the user can't infinite-pan away from the wheel: new `camera.setPanBounds(x0, y0, x1, y1)` API, view layer calls it after every `fitToExtent` with a half-worldSpan margin (generous breathing room), camera's `panByScreen` + animation `tick` both clamp the center. The pan-bounds feature was a side-question John raised ("we don't want infinite pan, is it limited right?") — wasn't, now is. Cache-bust `20260519-resize-snap-fix` (16 occurrences). Files: `src/js/app.js`, `src/js/engine/camera.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `index.html`. **Deferred to next batch**: glyph z-stacking bug (per-node SVG glyphs overflow into adjacent node circles) + selected-node glow visible-clipping-mask artifact (square quad boundary visible because neighboring disk quads discard fragments). Both need shader-level fixes; out of scope for this surgical commit. Note: Lane A agent `goblin-sacred-architecture-1` is concurrently active per `00_meta/ACTIVE-CONTENT.md`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-fade-gradient-v2` — three follow-ups on John's first-look feedback — FINISHED 2026-05-19.** John tested the fade-gradient commit (`4b0cec9`) and reported: (a) no visible gradient on wires, (b) no perceivable hover fade, (c) resize re-centers + zooms-out to fit (unwanted). Three fixes: **(a) Gradient cranked** from `mix(1.0, 0.55, in.edge_t)` → `mix(1.0, 0.25, in.edge_t)` in the edge fragment shader. 45% darken at the target end was lost on faint idle-alpha wires; 75% darken pushes them to near-black at the far end — direction now unmissable. **(b) Fade duration bumped** `FADE_DURATION = 0.10s` → `0.25s`. 100ms apparently fell below John's perception threshold (or the rAF cadence on his Mac is throttled enough to miss the 6-frame window); 250ms gives clear ease without feeling sluggish. Tunable from one constant in `forge.js`. **(c) Resize stops re-fitting** — removed `camera.fitToExtent` from `resizeAndFit` sizeChanged branch. User's zoom + pan state survives a viewport resize; only canvas pixel size changes, world coords stay anchored. `rebakeNodes()` still runs to keep screen-px clamps correct (camera.state.scale is preserved, just viewport changed). Cache-bust `20260519-fade-gradient-v2` (16 occurrences in `index.html`). Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `index.html`. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-fade-gradient` — 100ms wire fade-in/out on hover + universal source→target gradient on every wire — FINISHED 2026-05-19.** Two visual upgrades John requested after the pack-scale-fix landed. **(1) Hover/lock fade**: `local.edgeStates` (the per-edge state buffer the shader reads to mix idle/active color + width) is now the LIVE-ANIMATING buffer; a new `local.edgeTargets` holds the snap-to value computed by `recomputeFocus` from the focused set. `tickEdgeFades(dt)` in `animTick` advances states toward targets at `FADE_DURATION = 0.1s` (~6 frames at 60Hz). The shader was already doing `mix(idle, hot, state)` so fractional state values just-work — no shader change needed for the fade. `rebakeEdges` (dev-panel edge-param mutations) was also reworked to update TARGETS instead of snap-replacing states, so a mid-fade param tweak doesn't kill the animation. **(2) Universal wire gradient**: every wire (all 7 buckets, idle AND active) now darkens from its source endpoint toward its target endpoint. Added `edge_t` varying in the WGSL VsOut (carries the bezier `t` parameter computed in vs_main, 0 at source / 1 at target) → fragment shader multiplies color.rgb by `mix(1.0, 0.55, in.edge_t)` for a 45% darken at the target end. Provides eye-readable edge direction + helps separate visually-overlapping wires sharing an endpoint. dumpBugState gained `edgeTargets` + `animRafActive` fields for future fade-debug. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260519-fade-gradient`). Caveat: preview iframe throttles rAF so the fade itself was not directly verifiable in the harness — only the gradient + bake-state + pack-scale-fix were visually confirmed. The fade animation will run at 60Hz in John's foreground browser. Lane B slot OPEN.

> **`opus-forge-pack-scale-fix` — root cause of "tiny dots on load + after resize" found and fixed — FINISHED 2026-05-19.** The bug class that survived Option B, the convention-flip, and the cache-bust diagnostics turned out to be a **camScale-at-pack-time invariant violation**. `packNodes` (in `src/js/engine/graph/node.js:126-131`) bakes the world radius using the camera scale at pack time so the on-screen-px clamp can be evaluated. Two places were violating this: (1) `rebuildForMode` called `packNodes` BEFORE `fitToExtent`, so the first pack used the camera's default scale=1.0 while the camera then shrank to the actual fit scale (~0.4 for a 663-node deities wheel in a wide viewport) — every disk drew at the wrong on-screen size; (2) `resizeAndFit` (sizeChanged branch) re-fit the camera and rebaked edges + focus state, but never called `rebakeNodes()` — so after every resize the cached wrong-scale radii survived. The `camera.onChange` listener has a 5%-drift guard that fires `rebakeNodes()` automatically, which is why John saw "snap to correct after first cursor move" — coincidence of his gesture crossing the threshold, not by design. Three states explained: **State 1 (load)** = first pack at scale=1.0, drawn at scale=0.4 → tiny; **State 2 (cursor move)** = some user gesture eventually crosses 5% drift → corrective `rebakeNodes()` fires → correct; **State 3 (resize)** = re-fit changes scale again but rebake hook may not trip → wrong-scale radii survive → tiny again. His "snaps when I resize smaller" observation = small resizes that *also* happen to cross 5% drift get the corrective rebake by coincidence. Two surgical edits in `src/js/views/forge.js`: move the `ext` computation + `camera.fitToExtent` call to BEFORE `packNodes` in `rebuildForMode`; add `rebakeNodes()` to the sizeChanged branch of `resizeAndFit`. Cache-bust `20260519-pack-scale-fix` (17 occurrences in `index.html`). Lane B slot OPEN. **For John:** hard-refresh `?view=forge` — load should now show your tuned step-02 bake from frame zero, resize should preserve it. (Side note: your URL bar shows `file:///...` — per `feedback_atlas_needs_http_server.md`, please use `http://localhost:8742` via `start-atlas.command`; this bug is independent of that but the file:// path will bite us on PMTiles + service worker behavior in other views.)

> **`opus-forge-edge-state-invariant` — Flipped edge-state convention so 0 = IDLE (safe default), killing the "resize → all wires HOT" bug class — FINISHED 2026-05-18.** Followed `AUDIT/forge-edge-state-invariant-2026-05-18.md`. The bug had survived two earlier patch attempts (`opus-forge-phase-5d` + `opus-forge-phase-6d3`) because each fix only closed one downstream path — the root invariant violation was untouched. The edge-state convention was asymmetric with the node convention: nodes used `0 = no-dim` (zero-init = safe default), edges used `0 = HOT` (zero-init = every wire lit). Any GPU code path that allocated an edge-state buffer and didn't explicitly overwrite every slot with 1.0 painted every wire HOT. Fixed structurally with 3 surgical edits: (1) `adjacency.js:computeEdgeStates` now returns `1.0` for HOT and `0.0` for IDLE, with `if (!focusedSet) return out` (all-zeros = all-idle); (2) edge shader's three `mix()` arg orders flipped — `world_w_raw = mix(inst_extra.x, inst_extra.w, inst_state)` (idle→hot), `z = mix(0.85, 0.75, inst_state)` (idle behind, hot forward), `color = mix(in.edge_color, hot, in.state)`, `dim_mult = mix(1.0 - v.dim_amount, 1.0, in.state)`; (3) `views/forge.js` rebuildForMode dropped its `.fill(1.0)` — zero-init is now correct by construction. Verified live: cold idle (faint slate), Zeus locked (1-hop network bright), Zeus locked + resize (no orange flash, state preserved through pane size change). Side-nav badge p6d3 → p6d4. Cache-bust `20260518-forge-edge-state-invariant`. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/adjacency.js`, `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html`. See audit doc for full analysis.

> **`opus-forge-phase-6` — Forge dev panel UX + IDLE/ACTIVE wire split + label hierarchy + zoom-aware nodes — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** Per John's "make the dev panel REALLY good" brief, a comprehensive operator surface. WIRES restructured into nested collapsible groups: IDLE STATE (color + opacity + stroke per bucket × 7), ACTIVE STATE (color + opacity + stroke per bucket × 7), SHAPE (curvature per bucket — state-independent), FOCUS DIM. NODES gained on-screen-px clamps (min 3 / max 28 px) — camera re-packs nodes when scale drifts >5% so deep zoom-out keeps tiny dots legible and deep zoom-in doesn't bloat hubs. LABELS gained proper hierarchy: per-tier zoom thresholds (tier 0 always; tier 3 never by default) + screen-space AABB collision pruning that walks tiers top-down so hubs anchor. GLOBAL PALETTE section writes 3 CSS vars (--forge-bg, --forge-label-text, --forge-label-halo). Engine changes: `graph/edge.js` packEdges accepts `idleColors` + `hotWidths`, hot stroke now per-bucket per-instance (slot [11] replaces old pad); `graph/node.js` packNodes accepts `camScale` + `minScreenPx` + `maxScreenPx`; `graph/label.js` (new) `computeIdleLabelVisibility` runs per-tier zoom threshold + AABB pruning; `renderer/webgpu.js` edge shader replaces global `mix(2.4, 1.0, state)` hot-width hardcode with `mix(inst_extra.w, inst_extra.x, inst_state)` reading per-bucket widths from the instance attribute. `dev-panel-forge.js` full rewrite (LS_KEY bumped to v2): nested groups support, color picker control type with hex swatch, 8 sections / 8 groups / **57 sliders / 17 color rows**. All param ids cleaned up: `edge_idle_X` → `idle_opacity_X`, `edge_width_X` → `idle_stroke_X`, `edge_hot_X` → `active_opacity_X`, `hot_width_mult` split into per-bucket `active_stroke_X`. `views/forge.js` PARAM_DEFAULTS rewritten with new helpers (edgeOverridesFromParams, nodeOverridesFromParams, labelHierarchyFromParams); `setParam` accepts both numbers + hex strings; `camera.onChange` triggers node re-pack on scale drift; `syncLabels` now unions focus + idle-tier sets and uses the collision module; hitNodes carry their tier. Verified live: idle wheel shows faint slate atmosphere + hub labels ("Zeus", "Ra", "Astarte", "The One (Plotinus)", "Hermes Trismegistos", "Jesus Christ", "Mary (Theotokos)" etc.) collision-pruned; locking Zeus → focused 1-hop network lights up bright + labels appear + background dims; color picker mutation (idle_color_transmission → #00ff00) propagates immediately to bright green wires across the wheel; double-click row resets that slider. Side-nav badge p5 → p6. Files: `src/js/engine/dev-panel-forge.js` (full rewrite), `src/js/engine/graph/label.js` (new), `src/js/engine/graph/{edge,node}.js`, `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p6`).

Right-edge drawer (380 px, slides on the 'F' key or click the vertical "Visual ▸" tab). **41 sliders across 7 sections** with dummy-proof labels (the WHAT in plain English) + one-sentence hints (the WHAT MOVING IT DOES): WHAT YOU SEE AT REST (7 edge-bucket idle alphas + stage atmosphere), WHEN YOU HOVER OR LOCK (dim amount, hot-edge width multiplier, 6 per-bucket hot alphas), NODE CIRCLES (4 tier radii), SYMBOLS INSIDE EACH NODE (glyph scale + opacity + tint), WIRE SHAPE (7 widths + 4 curve strengths), LABELS (label size + cap), CAMERA FEEL (pan_tau + zoom_tau + flyto_dur). **Double-click a slider row → resets that one to its default**; "Reset all" button at the header resets everything. **Icon picker section**: 17 node types × 55-icon library (`src/js/engine/icon-library.js` — circle / ring / triangle / cross / star / sun / crescent / hexagram / pillar / scales / chalice / serpent / scroll / column / etc., grouped GEOMETRIC / STARS / SACRED / NATURE / OBJECT / ARCHITECTURAL / ABSTRACT). > **`opus-forge-phase-4f` — Forge search + cinematic fly-to — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** Type a deity's name, press Enter, watch the camera glide to frame their 1-hop neighborhood. Three additions: (1) `camera.flyTo({centerX, centerY, scale}, duration)` in `src/js/engine/camera.js` — sets a new `flyToAnim` state (from/to/duration/elapsed); the camera's `tick(dt)` advances it with ease-out cubic (`1 - (1 - t)^3` — fast start, soft landing); any existing pan-inertia/zoom-ease is cancelled (fly-to is a deliberate direction, not a momentum hand-off). (2) Search input in the status strip (right edge after a flex spacer), Enter commits / Esc clears. `findBestMatch(query)` walks the current mode's nodes and matches case-insensitive across `title`, `id`, and `aka` aliases; preference order is exact match > prefix match > substring; ties broken by degree (highest first) so "zeus" beats "zeusite" stubs. (3) `handleSearch(query)`: replaces lock with `{hitId}`, recomputes focus (1-hop expansion), then `flyToFocusedSet()` computes the world bbox of the focused nodes + padding and calls `camera.flyTo()` to frame it with margin. animation loop picks up via `startAnimLoop()`. Verified live: typing "zeus" in deities mode → status `LOCK 1`, camera animates from (0, 0, 1.20) → (-3.4, 40.7, 1.38) over ~33 frames (0.55s at 60fps), Zeus's Indo-European storm-god network (47 nodes across Greek, Hittite, Canaanite, Vedic, Egyptian, Chinese, Andean, Celtic, Roman, Slavic, Christian) renders lit + labeled + framed perfectly. Frame time 0.3 ms. Pantheon V2 view-change unaffected. Side-nav badge p4e → p4f. Files: `src/js/engine/camera.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p4f`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-4e` — Forge type-shape glyphs: one symbol per node type — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** Every disk now wears its type signature: ring-with-inner-dot for deities, head+shoulders for authors, page-with-lines for documents, four-point star for symbols, diamond-with-dot for events, plus for rituals, quarter-note for music, capital-A for alphabets, triangle for alchemy, open-circle for philosophy, scales for morals, staff for medicine, circle-cross for mathematics, pillar for monuments, dashed-ring for themes, ring-with-dot for traditions, pin for places. New `src/js/engine/graph/glyph.js` declares the 17-type SVG vocabulary as inner `<svg viewBox="0 0 12 12">` markup using `currentColor` so a wrapper element's `color` style drives the paint. Ported the first-pass primitives from `src/js/views/pantheon-v2.js` TYPE_GLYPHS — Forge ships visually consistent with existing Pantheon while we build. Forge view (`src/js/views/forge.js`) gained a `.forge-glyphs-overlay` div between canvas and labels (z-index 4); `rebuildForMode` populates one `<span class="forge-glyph">` per node containing the inline SVG, tinted to a lighter hue of the family color via `lightenColor(fc, 0.55)`. `syncGlyphPositions` runs on every drawFrame and positions each span at the node's screen position, sized to the disk diameter at current camera scale. pointer-events: none so glyphs never block hover. Verified live: Deities mode shows ring-with-dot on every disk; Documents shows page-rect; Music shows quarter-note. Glyph count tracks instance count exactly (663 / 422 / 108 for the modes tested). Frame time 0.0–0.3 ms across modes. Pantheon V2 view-change unaffected. Side-nav badge p4d → p4e. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/glyph.js` (new), `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p4e`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-4d` — Forge mode switching: 17 wheels in one app — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** Forge becomes a multi-modal viewer. New `src/js/engine/graph/mode.js` declares the 17 modes (deities / authors / documents / symbols / events / rituals / music / alphabet / alchemy / philosophy / morals / medicine / mathematics / monuments / themes / traditions / places) each with id / label / glyph / nodeType. `filterNodesByMode(mode, nodes, edges)` does simple type-filter for 16 modes; the `authors` mode is special — resolves persons who actually authored a document via `edge.type === 'authored'` (without the gate, every historical figure including monarchs/prophets shows up). View module refactored: data prep is now mode-dependent state on `local.mode` (`nodes`, `edges`, `positions`, `adjacency`, `nodePacked`, `edgePacked`, `hitNodes`, `worldExtent`); a new `rebuildForMode(modeId)` filters → lays out → packs → builds adjacency → resizes hit-test index → clears hover/lock → wipes label DOM nodes → resets camera fit. Mode dropdown sits FIRST in the status strip (the primary "what is this wheel" indicator). Custom-caret native `<select>` styled to match the chrome (mono font, gold-soft caret, gold-on-hover/focus). Verified live: dropdown round-trips all 17 modes — deities 663 / authors 211 / documents 422 / symbols 278 / events 191 / rituals 105 / music 108 / alphabet 41 / alchemy 31 / philosophy 9 / mathematics 8 / monuments 0 / themes 339 / traditions 169 / places 0 / deities 663 (back). Frame time 0.0–0.1 ms across all modes. Camera re-fits to each mode's world extent. Pantheon V2 view-change unaffected. Side-nav badge stays p4. Files: `src/js/engine/graph/mode.js` (new), `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p4d`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-4c` — Forge cinematic camera (pan inertia + zoom easing) — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** The camera stops snapping and starts gliding. Two independent animation systems live in `src/js/engine/camera.js`: (1) `panAnim` — CSS-px-per-second velocity that exponentially decays with `PAN_TAU = 0.18s` and stops at `PAN_STOP_THRESHOLD = 2 px/s`; (2) `zoomAnim` — target scale + screen anchor + world anchor that critically-damped eases with `ZOOM_TAU = 0.08s`, recomputing centre each tick to keep the world-point-under-the-cursor pinned throughout the ease. Both ticked by a single `camera.tick(dt)` that returns whether motion is still in flight. View layer (`src/js/views/forge.js`) gained: (a) a 6-sample ring buffer of pointer positions during drag, (b) release-velocity computation from the most recent ~80ms of samples (oldest-newest window so a final micro-move doesn't spike the estimate), (c) `kickPanVelocity(vx, vy)` call on `pointerup` with momentum, (d) `nudgeZoomTarget(factor, anchor, viewport)` replacing the direct `zoomAt` for wheel events so rapid scrolls compound smoothly instead of jerking discretely, (e) an rAF-driven animation loop (`startAnimLoop` + `animTick`) that runs only while motion is in flight and self-cancels on settle — no idle rAF burn. Pointerdown calls `camera.stopAnim()` so starting a new drag cancels any in-flight inertia from a previous release. Setup teardown cancels the rAF id + calls `camera.stopAnim()`. **Three details tuned during verification**: (1) `ev.timeStamp` is unreliable on synthetic PointerEvents; switched to `performance.now()` in pan-sample timestamps; (2) preview iframe clamps `setTimeout` to ~1s in background tabs, so automated tests can't reproduce a fast drag — added a `kickPanVelocity` debug hook so the animation math can be verified directly (bypassing pointer events) and confirmed the exponential decay curve: 1500 px/s → -19.86 → -69.87 → -108.18 → -137.52 → -159.99 → -177.21 → -190.39 → -200.49 → -208.22 → -214.14 over 30 frames at 16ms; (3) the previous Phase 3 verification's broken zoom-ease re-tested clean — 1.208 → 1.732 over 30 frames with cursor-anchor preserved. Side-nav badge stays p4. Files: `src/js/engine/camera.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p4c4`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-4b` — Forge lock-on-click + focused labels — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** The wheel becomes a reader. Click any deity → it pins to a `lockedSet` (separate from hover state), and the focused set + bright incident edges + labels for the 1-hop neighborhood persist when the cursor moves away. Click empty space → clears the entire lock. Click an already-locked deity → removes it. Labels render as DOM `<div>` overlays in a new `.forge-labels-overlay` div above the canvas; pointer-events: none so they never intercept hit-tests. Created lazily (one per node, on first show) and positioned via `camera.worldToScreen` each frame. Visibility is JS-driven: only nodes in the focused set get `display: block`. Halo (4-corner text-shadow) keeps labels readable over the hot-edge wire constellation. Cap at 80 visible labels for now (deconfliction is Phase 4c). Status strip gained a `lock` count. `focusedSetFor()` in `engine/graph/adjacency.js` updated to expand locked nodes through the adjacency map (same 1-hop rule as hover), so a click-pin preserves the network across the cursor leaving. Three rendering bugs caught in verification: (1) `setHoverId` was using `local.NODES_BY_ID.get(id)` but NODES_BY_ID is a plain object — defensive lookup added; (2) labels' CSS default `display: none` over-rode `el.style.display = ''` — explicit `'block'` assignment; (3) lock initially didn't expand neighbors so click + hover-off left only the locked node lit — adjacency expansion added to locked path. **Plus an AGENTS.md doctrine update**: new rule 5 in Craft doctrine — "Color, stroke, and visual style are a single locked system" — no per-element ad-hoc color tweaks during functional work, visual debt is recorded for a dedicated dogma batch, dev panel built as part of that batch covers ALL visual params. Verified live: click Zeus → status `LOCK 1`, move cursor away → 47 labels persist (Zeus + 46 1-hop neighbors across Greek/Hittite/Canaanite/Vedic/Egyptian/Chinese/Andean/Celtic/Roman/Slavic/Christian/Mesoamerican). Frame time 0.0 ms. Pantheon V2 view-change unaffected. Files: `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/js/engine/graph/adjacency.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `AGENTS.md`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p4b2`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-4a` — Forge hot-edge brighten on focus — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** The wires light up. Hovering Zeus now paints the ~40 transmission / fusion / kinship / attestation edges incident to him in their bucket-hex colors at hot alpha (~0.85–0.95), instead of leaving them at slate idle. Hot edges also widen (2.4× the idle stroke) so the 1-hop ribbon reads as the dominant visual against the dimmed background. View-uniform extended from 32 → 160 bytes: existing header (view_scale / view_offset / viewport_px / dim_amount / pad) + a new `bucket_hot_colors: [8]vec4` array uploaded once on bootstrap with the 7-bucket palette from `window.EDGE_BUCKETS`. Edge fragment shader reads bucket_index from the per-instance attribute (already in inst_extra.z), looks up the hot color, and mixes idle ↔ hot by state: `color = mix(hot, instance_color, state)` so state=0 (focused) → fully hot, state=1 (dim) → idle slate × dim_amount. New `renderer.setBucketPalette(colors)` helper lets a future dev-panel re-color buckets live without re-baking the wheel. Verified live: hover Zeus at canvas (1114, 1110) — amber/orange transmission edges radiate from Zeus's Greek cluster to Egyptian (Ra/Amun), Mesopotamian (Marduk), Vedic (Indra/Dyaus Pita), Hittite (Teshub), Canaanite (Baal-Hadad), Norse (Thor), Chinese (Shangdi), Christian/Gnostic — the iconic Indo-European storm-god transmission web in one frame. Frame time 0.2 ms. Pantheon V2 view-change unaffected. Side-nav badge p3 → p4. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p4a`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-3-fix` — Forge pan inverted + hover offset — FIXED 2026-05-17.** Phase 3 shipped with a coordinate-system mismatch: the renderer's view-uniform already applies `viewScaleY = -cam.scale*2/vp.h` (flipping math-Y-up world to screen-Y-down framebuffer), and during Phase 3 verification I incorrectly added a *second* Y-flip to `camera.screenToWorld`/`worldToScreen`/`panByScreen`/`zoomAt`. Net effect: the camera thought Zeus was at the top of the canvas (cssY ≈ 252) while the renderer painted him at the bottom (cssY ≈ 1110) — hover at cursor returned the wrong node, and pan-down moved the wheel up. Reverted `camera.js` to a straight world↔screen affine (no flip in the camera). The renderer keeps its single flip. Verified live: hovering at canvas (1114, 1110) — where Zeus actually paints — returns "Zeus"; at the old broken position (1114, 252) returns "Baal-Hadad" (Mesopotamian, opposite side of wheel — confirming the flip-inversion). Drag (+200,+100) → camera center → (-165, -83) (both negative, world content follows cursor). Pan: ✓. Zoom-to-cursor: ✓ (anchor preserved). Hover screenshot shows Zeus's Greek cluster lit at the wheel's actual bottom-center plus 1-hop relations across Egyptian / Vedic / Chinese / Hittite / Canaanite / Mesopotamian families. Files: `src/js/engine/camera.js`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p3e`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-3` — Forge camera (pan/zoom) + hover state (1-hop highlight + dim) — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** The wheel becomes a tool. Two new engine modules: `src/js/engine/camera.js` (camera state {centerX, centerY, scale} with Y-flipped world↔screen math matching the renderer's view-uniform; `panByScreen`, `zoomAt(factor, anchor, viewport)` for cursor-anchored zoom that keeps the world point under the cursor invariant, `fitToExtent` for initial frame; subscribers via `onChange`), `src/js/engine/graph/adjacency.js` (`buildAdjacency(edges)` → Map<id, Set<id>>, `focusedSetFor(hover, locked, adj)` → focused-id Set, `computeNodeStates` + `computeEdgeStates` → Float32Array of per-instance dim states). Renderer (`engine/renderer/webgpu.js`) refactored: `drawFrame` now takes `camera` instead of `worldExtent`, computes the view-uniform from camera per frame. Two new per-instance VBOs (`nodeStateVbo`, `edgeStateVbo`, single float each) plumbed through both pipelines; shaders read state and multiply alpha by `mix(1, 1-dimAmount, state)` so focused instances stay at full alpha and the rest fade. Forge view (`src/js/views/forge.js`) wires pointermove → hit-test (CPU O(N) distance² < radius² against world-space packed nodes), pointerdown/up → camera pan, wheel → camera zoom-toward-cursor, camera.onChange → drawFrame, all event-driven (no rAF loop — Chrome throttles rAF in the preview iframe). Status strip gained `hover` field. **Three bugs caught and fixed during verification**: (1) camera math missing Y-flip (layout stores math-Y-up; renderer flips Y; camera screenToWorld/worldToScreen need to flip too — fixed in camera.js); (2) `setHoverId` assumed `window.NODES_BY_ID` is a Map but it's a plain object — throws and kills DOM update silently (defensive lookup added); (3) `setPointerCapture` throws on untrusted synthetic events in Chromium — wrapped in try (capture is a UX nicety for real input, not a correctness gate). Verified live: hover at Zeus's screen position → `HOVER: Zeus`, wheel dims to ~15% with Zeus's 1-hop neighborhood (Greek + Roman counterparts + Egyptian sun-gods + Vedic Indra/Dyaus Pita + Hittite/Canaanite + Chinese Shangdi + Mesopotamian Marduk) lit up across all families. Pan drag of 200×100 px shifts camera center from (0,0) → (-165, 82). Wheel one notch takes scale 1.208 → 1.732 with cursor anchor preserved. Frame time 0.2 ms. Pantheon V2 view-change retains all 3,033 edges + 663 labels — zero regression. Side-nav badge bumped p2 → p3. Files: `src/js/engine/camera.js` (new), `src/js/engine/graph/adjacency.js` (new), `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p3d`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-2` — Forge full wheel: 663 deities + 3,033 edges through the proprietary engine — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** The Pantheon's full information density now renders through the proprietary WebGPU pipeline. Three new engine modules: `src/js/engine/layout/radial.js` (pure-function port of pantheon-v2's wedge layout — family grouping, sqrt-weighted arc allocation, Fermat-spiral / golden-angle Vogel placement, configurable annulus + gap; ships with `computeDegree` and `filterEdgesByNodes` helpers), `src/js/engine/graph/node.js` (packs deities into a Float32Array instance buffer at 8 floats × 32 bytes per instance — `vec4(x, y, radius, pad)` + `vec4(rgba)` — with degree-tier radii `[18, 14, 11, 8]` and a cached color parser), `src/js/engine/graph/edge.js` (packs edges into 12 floats × 48 bytes per instance — endpoints + premultiplied color + width + curveStrength + bucketIndex — resolves bucket via `window.EDGE_BUCKET`, paints headline buckets at bucket-hue / others at slate atmosphere). The renderer (`engine/renderer/webgpu.js`) gained two pipelines: an instanced disk pipeline (shared 6-vertex quad VBO + per-instance attribute buffer, SDF AA via `fwidth`) and a curved-edge pipeline (33-segment ribbon mesh per edge, quadratic bezier sampled in the vertex shader with control point pulled toward the wheel centre by `curveStrength`, premultiplied-alpha blending). A shared view-uniform buffer carries the world→NDC affine for both pipelines; one `drawFrame({ viewportCss, worldExtent, nodeInstances, edgeInstances })` paints the entire wheel in one submission. Forge view (`src/js/views/forge.js`) reads `VAULT_DATA`, filters to deity-deity edges, computes degree + radial layout, packs instance buffers, async-bootstraps the renderer, fits to viewport. Status strip reports node count / edge count / per-frame ms. **Verified live**: 663 nodes + 3033 edges rendered in 1.7 ms per frame (target was <16 ms = 60 fps; achieved ~580 fps theoretical headroom). Family-colored disks arranged in radial wedges; edges curve as faint atmospheric threads through the wheel centre. Pantheon V2 view-change retains 3,033 edges + 663 labels — zero regression. Files: `src/js/engine/layout/radial.js` (new), `src/js/engine/graph/node.js` (new), `src/js/engine/graph/edge.js` (new), `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js`, `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p2`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-1` — Forge WebGPU bootstrap + first disk — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** The proprietary engine renders its first pixel. New `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` is the only file in the engine that knows about WebGPU APIs — implements adapter+device acquisition, context configuration with the preferred swap-chain format, WGSL shader compilation, vertex buffer (one reused 6-vertex quad), uniform buffer (12-float per-disk record matching a WGSL struct), bind-group layout, render pipeline with premultiplied-alpha blend, and a `drawDisk(pxX, pxY, pxR, color, viewportCss)` primitive that paints an anti-aliased SDF disk. SDF approach with `fwidth()`-derived AA gives sub-pixel-perfect edges at any zoom / DPR. The Forge view (`src/js/views/forge.js`) now mounts a status strip + full-area canvas, async-bootstraps the renderer, draws a single brand-gold (#d4a55a) disk at canvas centre sized 18 % of min(width, height). ResizeObserver re-fits + redraws on viewport change; teardown destroys the GPU device + buffers cleanly via `rootEl._engine.destroy`. **Initial-paint fix**: the Phase 1a version used `requestAnimationFrame` for the first draw — Chrome throttles rAF in hidden tabs (the preview iframe), so the first frame never fired. Phase 1b removes the rAF defer and paints synchronously after `await gpu.create()` (which already gives layout enough time to settle). Verified live: status strip reads "DEVICE active · SURFACE bgra8unorm · 1×dpr", anti-aliased gold disk renders dead-center on the Forge stage, Pantheon V2 view-change retains all 3,033 edges + 663 labels (no regression), Forge pane + canvas fully torn down on view-change (GPU device released). Side-nav badge bumped `p0` → `p1`. Files: `src/js/engine/renderer/webgpu.js` (new), `src/js/views/forge.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p1b`).

> **`opus-forge-phase-0` — Forge tab scaffold + Craft doctrine — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** Foundation slab for the proprietary WebGPU engine that will eventually replace Pantheon V2. Isolated route at `?view=forge`, same pattern V2 used: own pane (`.forge-pane`), own state, zero shared mutable state with the rest of the app, read-only access to `window.VAULT_DATA`. New `src/js/engine/` folder with three load-bearing files: `contract.js` (the Scene / Camera / Renderer API surface — the ONLY thing view modules see), `types.js` (visual style vocabulary: Style / Gradient / Transform / BlendMode / Shadow — hand-mirrored from the portable core's `types.ts` which mirrors the portable core Rust structs, so future (a)→(b) port via wgpu/Vello stays mechanical), `math.js` (affine transforms / world↔screen / bounds / bezier / color helpers — pure functions, ported from the portable core's `canvasGeometry.ts`). Engine `README.md` documents the architecture invariants: view layer never touches WebGPU directly, types match the portable core, no third-party libraries in `engine/`, performance targets (60 fps at 10k nodes, hover <8ms). Forge view (`src/js/views/forge.js`) is Phase 0 only — mounts an informational pane reporting engine module status, WebGPU availability, visible data counts, and the phase roadmap (0 Scaffold → 1 WebGPU bootstrap → 2 Full wheel → 3 Visual parity → 4 Timeline-forge → Graduation). No GPU device yet; that's Phase 1. **AGENTS.md gained a new "Craft doctrine" section** with four load-bearing rules: (1) we are NOT shipping until 100 % done, no "ship fast" posture, launch criterion is "competitors can't reproduce the design + visualization"; (2) standing obligation to re-evaluate tech at every friction signal — polishing wrong-tech instead of escalating is a protocol violation; (3) proprietary, not rented — no commercial/SaaS deps for the production rendering surface, MIT/BSD/Apache FOSS only at the primitive layer; (4) forward compatibility — engine contracts mirror the portable core by intent. Verified live: Forge tab visible in side nav with "p0" badge, all 3 engine modules load, WebGPU available, 3110 / 663 / 19043 nodes / deities / edges visible from VAULT_DATA, Pantheon V2 still mounts on view-change (regression-free). Files: `AGENTS.md`, `src/js/engine/{contract,types,math}.js` (new), `src/js/engine/README.md` (new), `src/js/views/forge.js` (new), `src/js/app.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-forge-p0`).

> **`opus-edge-buckets-universal-1` — Universal 7-bucket edge palette + ONTOLOGY-correct routing — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** Extracted `src/js/edge-buckets.js` as single source of truth for the 7 semantic edge buckets (Transmission / Parallel / Association / Kinship / Attestation / Polemic / Fusion). Both `pantheon-v2.js` and `app.js` now consume `window.edgeStyleFor(type)` from this module. **Routing fixes per ONTOLOGY.md §3 (master)**: (1) the `syncretic` family moved from Parallel → **Fusion** — `syncretic` (935), `syncretic-ancient-identification` (336), `syncretic-scholarly-parallel` (792), `syncretic-folk-syncretism` (22), `syncretic-identification`, `syncretic-syncretic-identification`, `syncretic-continuous-development`, `syncretic-aspect-of`, `syncretic-instantiation` — ~2,085 edges now paint amber instead of teal, restoring the canonical Fusion read (Inanna ↔ Ishtar ↔ Astarte ↔ Aphrodite ↔ Isis-Hellenistic). (2) Previously-unmapped types added: `heir-of` (60) + `descended-from` (18) + `syncretic-direct-influence` (15) → Transmission; `parallel` (89) + `convergence` (22) + `syncretic-cross-tradition-parallel` (39) → Parallel; `syncretic-mythological-partner` (23) + `syncretic-child-of` (10) + `sibling` (12) → Kinship; `member-of` + `syncretic-applies-to` + `syncretic-part-of` + `syncretic-shared-tradition` → Association. (3) `syncretic-lineage-claim` reclassified from Polemic → Transmission (lineage = ancestry, not hostile reframing). (4) Headline-at-idle opacities restored to AUDIT/edge-color-spec §6 (Polemic 0.25, Fusion 0.30, `ancestor-of` 0.30 override on Transmission). (5) Per-type width overrides preserved for production D3 Pantheon visual continuity (`ancestor-of` 0.46, `polemic-inversion` 0.46, symbol-* widths). Deleted the dormant legacy `EDGE_STYLE` block from `pantheon-v2.js` (per AUDIT proposal §5 — resurrection risk eliminated); replaced `app.js`'s per-type `EDGE_STYLE` map with a Proxy shim that delegates to `edgeStyleFor` so legacy `EDGE_STYLE[type]` indexers still work. Verified live: V2 Pantheon Deities mode renders **1,977 fusion edges** (was near-zero pre-patch; was visually invisible), Poseidon ↔ Neptune-Roman `syncretic` paints amber, console shows zero new errors, all 7 buckets allocated correctly across 3,033 visible edges. Files: `src/js/edge-buckets.js` (new), `src/js/views/pantheon-v2.js`, `src/js/app.js`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-edge-buckets-universal`). See `AUDIT/ontology-pantheon-bucket-routing-2026-05-17.md` for the rationale.

> **`opus-pantheon-nolabeltwitch-1` — Pantheon V2 label twitch during pan/zoom — FIXED 2026-05-17.** Regression introduced by the space-fill rule in `opus-pantheon-polish-1`: every camera update fired a debounced `updateNodeLabelVisibility` (80 ms mid-motion) which re-seeded `visibility:hidden` on non-hub labels — but `scheduleDeconflict` is gated by `_isPanning`, so deconflict couldn't re-promote them until the user released. Net effect: labels flickered out mid-motion and stayed out. Two surgical fixes. (1) `updateNodeLabelVisibility` now seeds visibility ONLY on transitions into the pool (display:none → ''); labels already in the pool keep their deconflict-assigned visibility. Active/hovered still lift from hidden. Invariant restored: deconflict owns the visibility property after first run. (2) The mid-motion `_camRefreshT = setTimeout(updateNodeLabelVisibility, 80)` schedule deleted entirely — pool is already wide in hub mode, so we don't lose anything by waiting for the 200 ms settle to re-evaluate threshold. Verified live: synthetic zoom + synthetic pan-drag both produce **0 visibility flips** across 662 labels. Files: `src/js/views/pantheon-v2.js`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-nolabeltwitch`).

> **`opus-pantheon-focus-vis-1` — Focus-state visual overhaul + dev-panel always-on — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** Four interlocking fixes addressing live feedback. (1) Dev panel access — removed the `?dev=1` URL gate from `src/js/dev-panel.js`. The vertical "Dev Panel" tab now appears on every page load (right edge); `D` key still toggles. The SaaS-pivot framing: tweak surface is John's operator tool, not a user feature, gated downstream at the auth boundary. (2) Type-glyph tint — every wheel-overlay glyph now picks up a *lighter hue of its own family color* (not stark white). New `lightenColor(hex, 0.55)` helper mixes the family hex toward `#fff`; the result is set inline as `style.color` per element at build time; `currentColor` inside the SVG paths inherits. CSS dropped the white `rgba(255,255,255,…)` color; the dev-panel `Type glyph opacity` slider now drives actual `opacity`. (3) Focus dim — new `applyTypeGlyphDim()` toggles a `.ph2-type-glyph-dim` class (opacity 0.10) on glyphs whose node is in the DIM state (hover or lock context active but this node is outside the focus set) or whose family is filtered out. Wired into both `applyEdgeHoverState` (all four return paths) and `applyHullFilterState`. (4) Edge z-order — split the single SVG overlay into two: hulls + ticks stay below sigma (so node disks paint on top of family backgrounds and `clickStage` hit-test for hulls still works), edges are lifted into a new `.ph2-edges-overlay` at `z-index: 2` so bright wires paint OVER the dim disks when a node is locked. Without the lift, hot wires read as faint threads behind a sea of node disks; with it, they're the dominant visual signal of the focused connection neighborhood. Verified live: clicking Zeus dims every non-focused disk to a soft family hue at 10% opacity, the radial wires to Jupiter/Apollo/Marduk/Baal-Hadad/Indra read clearly across faded Greek/Mesopotamian/Vedic clusters, and the dev tab is visible without any URL flag. Files: `src/js/views/pantheon-v2.js`, `src/js/dev-panel.js`, `src/styles/app.css`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-focusvis`).

> **`opus-pantheon-polish-1` — Pantheon V2 polish: music glyph + label space-fill + larger node tiers — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** Three Pantheon V2 fixes addressing live feedback. (1) Music type-glyph redrawn as a clean quarter-note (head + stem; matches the toolbar's `♩`); replaced the previous double-eighth design that read as noise at small sizes. (2) Smart-label space-fill rule: in `hub` label mode, every label now enters the deconflict pool — hubs claim their slots first (greedy first-fit by degree), then lower-tier labels are promoted to visible when their bbox doesn't collide with any already-claimed bbox. Empty-hull traditions (Christian / Macedonian / Pre-Islamic Arabian / Pacific…) now surface dozens of secondary labels they previously hid; Music mode in particular reads as a fully labeled wheel because each family has only 3–10 nodes with abundant breathing room. (3) Node tier-radii bumped from `[13,10,7,5]` → `[16,12,9,7]` — the rest-tier (low-degree single nodes in sparse hulls) is now 40 % bigger and clearly legible; biggest hubs grow proportionally so the degree hierarchy stays visible. Verified live: Deities wheel shows visibly larger disks + many more labels in sparse hulls; Music wheel shows the new quarter-note glyph on every disk + full descriptive labels for every node; hover card title row uses the new music glyph. Files: `src/js/views/pantheon-v2.js`, `index.html` (cache-bust `20260517-polish`).

> **`opus-pantheon-hashrouter-1` — Pantheon V2 hash router + dev-panel knobs — FINISHED 2026-05-17.** URL is now the single source of view state. Five params round-trip cleanly: `view` (top-level route), `mode` (deities / authors / documents / …), `families` (CSV of selected family names), `locked` (CSV of locked node ids), `focus` (single selected node). Mode-change / family-filter toggle / hull-click / node-click / empty-stage-reset all replaceState. Cross-view navigation pushState so back/forward moves between views. Renamed each conflict pair so deity keeps bare slug, symbol/person gets `-symbol`/`-person` suffix; surgically redirected the inbound wikilinks that semantically meant the renamed entity. **6 more cross-folder dupes** caught by the new hard-fail (ark-of-the-covenant, divine-twins, dying-rising-god, garuda, janus, kalachakra) — symbol gets `-symbol` suffix, theme/deity keeps bare slug. **5 scribal same-slug dupes merged** (inanna ↔ inanna-sumerian, ningal ↔ nin-gal-sumerian, usha ↔ ushas, yima-iranian ↔ yima-jamshid, holy-spirit-paraclete ↔ holy-spirit) — unique content folded into the richer keeper, inbound wikilinks redirected, loser deleted. **Classifier hardened:** 9 fragile substrings word-boundaried (`\bbon\b`, `\bzen\b`, `\binca\b`, `\biberian\b`, `\bmaya\b`, `\bshang\b`, `\bbreton\b`, `\bakan\b`, `\bsan\b`) — closes the substring-collision bug class (Bonaventure/Sorbonne, Renaissance/citizen, incarnation/incantation, Siberian, Maya Angelou, Shanghai, André Breton, Arakanese, Pisan civic cult). New guards: `tewahedo`/`apostolic`/`orthodox` → Christian *before* African/Armenian/Greek; `not s.startswith("greek")` on Hittite; `greco-roman`/`graeco-roman` → Greek; `levantine` → Canaanite (with pre-islamic guard); `caodai` no-space variant. Verified: 0 deities in Other, 33 named families, all 14 spot-check classifications pass in the live atlas, 8 lost deities now render on the Pantheon ring (Michael/Gabriel = Christian, Padmasambhava = Buddhist, Lamassu = Mesopotamian, etc.), 0 regressions on Chinese-origin deities, no new dead links introduced.
